# Interaction analytics implementation plan

Status: implementation underway, 2026-10-16. Analytics remains disabled. This document defines the full implementation and acceptance criteria; only the work explicitly recorded in §14 is implemented. Read it with [RULES.md](RULES.md) and [IMPLEMENTING.md](IMPLEMENTING.md). Proposed names below must enter the reviewed registry before use in code.

## 1. What we want to learn

The server already observes many completed operations. The client's useful contribution is the path before the operation: where the user started, which alternatives they tried, what blocked them, how they recovered, and where they stopped. A successful API call alone cannot answer these questions.

Every proposed measurement must identify a product question, a decision it can change, the minimum data needed, its denominator, its privacy scope, and a test. Do not instrument every control just because it exists. Screen inventory is a navigation audit, not proof that interaction scenarios are covered.

| Question | Minimum useful evidence | Possible product decision |
|---|---|---|
| Which media entry paths work well? | Starts and outcomes by attach, paste, drop, recording and picker route | Improve the routes with high local rejection or abandonment |
| Where does media preparation become difficult? | Preview steps, local failure category, retry, coarse preparation duration | Simplify editing, explain limits, improve conversion |
| Do people discover the sending options they need? | Entry into scheduling, silent send, file/media mode, followed by completion or cancellation | Improve labels and placement |
| How do people find chats and messages? | Entry surface, search/filter steps, selection or abandonment; no query or result content | Improve search and navigation |
| Are apparent failures recoverable? | Coarse failure followed by retry, alternate route, cancellation or accepted submission | Improve recovery actions and feedback |
| Can optional client evidence help abuse investigation? | Bounded aggregates of legitimate action paths and local rejection/retry patterns | Evaluate incremental value alongside independent server evidence |

Do not add another event for a server-visible fact unless its client context answers one of these questions. Prefer a journey summary over separate events for every intermediate step. Existing action events can remain for compatibility, but dashboards must not sum them with journey outcomes as separate sends.

Define reporting before adding events. Compare local acceptance, cancellation, abandonment and terminal failure by entry route; measure recovery among journeys that encountered a blocker, and preview completion among journeys that reached preview. These are ratios within eligible observed journeys, not all app usage. With terminal summaries alone, a lost/crashed unfinished journey is unobserved until eligible recovery exists; do not call the denominator “all starts.” An additional start event needs a specific justification. Segment by implemented client version and sampling policy, exclude unknown routes from route comparisons while reporting their share, and keep cancellations separate from errors. Privacy revocation and telemetry loss are missing data, never product failure or suspected abuse.

## 2. Initial findings before implementation

These are baseline source observations, not claims about completed fixes. See §14 for subsequent changes. The working tree contains unrelated edits; implementation must preserve them and recheck these entry points before changing them.

| Area | Evidence | Gap / implication |
|---|---|---|
| Enablement | `Telegram-Mac/app/AppDelegate.swift`, `applicationWillFinishLaunching` sets `Analytics.shared.isEnabled = false` | Keep it disabled. The hub itself defaults to enabled, so startup configuration is not a complete default-deny design. |
| Secret chats | `packages/AppAnalytics/Sources/AppAnalytics/Analytics.swift`, `sendRaw`, screen tracking and `PeerType.init` | No central exclusion; `secret_chat` is an accepted peer classification. Events without `peer_type` are equally important. |
| Media routes | `ChatUI/ChatController.swift`, `attachFile`, `attachPhotoOrVideo`, `attachPicture`, `draggingItems`, `showPreviewSender`, `sendMedia`, `sendMedias`; `ChatUI/ChatInputView.swift`, `processPaste`; `PreviewSenderUI/PreviewSenderController.swift`, `processPaste` | Routes converge without an analytics origin. A final callback cannot reconstruct paste versus drop versus attach. Paste can also open the rich text editor or add media to an existing preview. |
| Send meaning | `ChatController.sendAnalyticsMessage` and callers, including `sendMedias` | Some send events precede `Sender.enqueue`. They do not prove local acceptance or remote delivery. |
| Flow ownership | `AppAnalytics/AnalyticsFlow.swift` | Flow state is in memory; destruction emits abandonment. `init(engine:)` uses the shared hub rather than binding to that engine. Disabled collection does not prevent recording steps in memory. |
| Account ownership | `AppDelegate.contextValue`, `Analytics.setEngine` | A mutable global sink follows the active account. Delayed work needs immutable account ownership and revocation to prevent attribution to a different account. |
| Visibility | `AnalyticsScreenTracker.Visible`, `show`, `hide`, `showStandalone` | Entries retain a screen and start time, without privacy/account scope. Same screen identity short-circuits updates. Standalone panels and late hide callbacks need inherited scope. |
| Validation | `Analytics.validate` / `sendRaw` | Debug logging reports invalid payloads but still sends them. Enum/type validation is incomplete; `flow.end` skips the unknown-param check. |
| Legacy bypasses | Direct `addAppLogEvent` in `PreviewSenderUI`, `PremiumUI`, `PeerInfoUI` and `ChatUI/ChatServiceItem.swift` | The hub's switch does not govern these paths. In particular, image-editor logging is reachable through media preparation. Audit all callers, including core and the share extension. |
| Queue | TelegramCore `SynchronizeAppLogEventsOperation.swift` and `ManagedSynchronizeAppLogEventsOperations.swift` | Persistent Postbox queue; batches limited to 50 and approximately 256 KB with a 30-second delay. This is not a total queue bound, and a single oversized event can exceed the byte target. Existing queued events have no new policy provenance. |
| Existing coverage | `coverage/macos.yaml`, `AnalyticsHubTests.swift`, `FlowFixturesTests.swift` | Screen decisions and flow fixtures exist. There is no scenario ledger or secret-chat isolation suite. |

Paths abbreviated as `ChatUI/...` and `PreviewSenderUI/...` above are under `packages/<Module>/Sources/<Module>/`.

## 3. Non-negotiable privacy boundary

Secret-chat activity must produce **zero analytics collection, buffering, persistence, debug payloads or transmission**. This includes opening/creating a secret chat, message actions, media preview/editing, menus, pickers, profiles, gallery, search, alerts, recording, cancellation and failure. Never emit a replacement event such as `secret_chat_opened`, `suppressed`, or a secret-chat count. Analytics suppression must not disable functional messaging or security protocols.

Use layered enforcement:

1. Introduce an explicit local scope for every producer: account ownership, policy generation, and classification such as ordinary, excluded or unresolved. These are local control data, not payload fields. Resolve secret peers from the chat location/namespace as early as possible, before asynchronous peer loading. Unknown or ambiguous scope is denied; absence of a peer is not evidence that an event is global.
2. Carry scope through navigation, hooks, actions and asynchronous callbacks. A child inherits its initiating scope; independently resolved destination restrictions can only tighten it. A secret-derived action remains excluded even after the original screen disappears. Any secret source or destination excludes a mixed action as a whole.
3. As a conservative initial policy, suspend all product analytics while any secret-chat surface or its dependent surface is visible in any app window. Suspend unscoped lifecycle/menu/shortcut producers as well. A window-local policy may be considered only after isolation is proven and explicitly reviewed.
4. Gate before starting a recorder, before allocating payloads or deriving analytics-only attributes, before debug output, at enqueue, and at batch dispatch. Recheck the policy generation at the actual serialized admission point; a copied sink must not escape a concurrent disable or account switch.
5. On entry to an excluded context, silently invalidate active analytics journeys and visibility state that could span it. Do not emit final abandonment, hide, duration, interruption or suppression events. On return, start clean eligible state without a backfilled source or elapsed private interval. Rotate analytics session state without a special transition event.
6. Privacy revocation is terminal for a token. A delayed completion, flow deinit, picker cancellation, account switch, or later re-enable cannot revive it. Reset counters and previous-screen history as well as visible entries.
7. Global disable/opt-out prevents collection and dispatch and purges pending product analytics, including unfinished snapshots. Entry into secret UI also purges previously admitted ordinary batches and cancels pending analytics requests. This conservative policy deliberately loses ordinary observations instead of retaining state across a private interval. Purge ambiguous or unscoped legacy queue entries before any future enablement. An already dispatched ordinary batch cannot be recalled; no private-context event may enter it.
8. Route reachable legacy calls through the same policy or suppress them. Legacy server compatibility does not override secret-chat exclusion. Keep a reviewed bypass inventory with a reason and test for every transport caller.

The wire registry may retain `secret_chat` and secret-screen identifiers for compatibility, but clients must never emit them. Do not remove enum values silently or mistake a registry name for permission to collect. A payload filter for `peer_type == secret_chat` is insufficient because many events have no peer field.

Keep all existing content restrictions: no text, captions, queries, clipboard contents, file names/paths, URLs, phone numbers, peer/message identifiers, or content hashes. Do not add exact pointer coordinates, key sequences, per-keystroke timing, cross-session identifiers, device fingerprints, source application names or clipboard polling. Read only the coarse route already known to the handler. `peer = 0` is not anonymity: transport uses an authorized account.

## 4. Model a user's task, not a callback

A journey can cross several controllers. Own it by the user task, with one local token propagated through existing feature hooks; do not depend on the globally current screen or a global “last input method.” Existing single-screen flows remain supported, subject to the privacy gate.

Keep these dimensions separate, with bounded registry enums:

| Proposed dimension | Meaning | Examples / limits |
|---|---|---|
| `entry_point` | Product route that began the task | `attach_menu`, `clipboard`, `drop_target`, `media_picker`, `recorder`, `share_extension`, `draft_resume`, `unknown` |
| Existing `via` | How that entry action was invoked | Existing supported enum values such as `click`, `shortcut`, `drag_drop`; do not infer keyboard entry merely from a pasteboard callback |
| `source` | Eligible initiating screen | Capture explicitly at entry; a global visibility stack is insufficient with multiple windows |
| `submit_via` | How the final submission was requested | Proposed `send_control`, `enter`, `cmd_enter`, `send_menu`, `unknown`; distinct from acquisition route |
| `media_mode` | How accepted attachments will be sent | Draft `media`, `file`, `archive`, `mixed`; separate from photo/video kind |
| `destination_kind` | Coarse chat context | Ordinary peer type plus bounded history/thread/scheduled mode; no peer identity or topic title |
| Outcome and reason | Local terminal boundary and coarse blocker | Explicit cancel, close/abandon, terminal failure, local submission accepted; reasons such as permission, unsupported format, size limit, preparation failure |
| Duration/count buckets | Effort and scale needed for a decision | Coarse elapsed/preparation time, attachment count, retries; define zero buckets where zero is a valid result |

These are proposals, not new permitted params. Do not change the meaning of existing `source`, `via`, `success` or `chat.message.send`. If existing semantics cannot express local submission clearly, add a separately named event or flow with a documented terminal boundary and deprecate the ambiguous use through registry review.

Suggested state model: eligible start → acquisition → preparation/preview → submit attempt → local acceptance. Acquisition/preview can repeat. Recoverable rejection returns to an active state; explicit cancel, abandonment and terminal failure end it. Exclusion revokes it silently from every state. At most one terminal summary is emitted.

Record actual choices and meaningful system responses, not renders, hover, every edit or every retry callback. For initial attach followed by paste into preview, preserve the first entry and record an `add_via_paste` step; never rewrite the first entry. Mixed batches retain one journey with bounded route/category steps. Use the existing step cap as a starting bound, with bucketed timing for new journeys; exact timings in existing flows require a separate compatibility decision.

Record an empty picker cancellation as cancellation, not an empty send. A submission that fails before local acceptance stays retryable. Once local acceptance succeeds, end the composition journey; remote upload/delivery retries belong to a separate recovery journey only if they answer a client UX question. Scheduling succeeds at local acceptance of the scheduled request, not when it is eventually delivered. A caption/additional text, album splitting or repeated completion must not create duplicate journey outcomes.

Initially keep journey tokens local; one summary already connects the steps. Do not add persistent IDs or transmit message identifiers to join client paths to server requests. If a server correlation token is later necessary, specify its narrow purpose, lifetime, payload contract and privacy review first.

## 5. First implementation slice: media journeys

Each row becomes a scenario-ledger entry with exact initiating handler, propagated context, terminal owner, expected trace, negative trace and test. [The macOS interaction ledger](coverage/macos-interactions.md) now maps the first 22 media routes to source handlers; all remain end-to-end incomplete and UI-unverified; the ledger distinguishes partial source wiring from native evidence. “Candidate” means verify availability before promising coverage; unsupported routes must be marked not applicable with evidence.

| Scenario | Important variants and checks |
|---|---|
| Attach files | Menu versus keyboard entry; one/many files; directories; empty/cancelled picker; invalid/all rejected/partially accepted selection |
| Attach photo/video | Photo-only, video-only, mixed; media versus file; size limit; preview failure |
| Drop onto chat | Media target versus file target; direct-send bypass versus preview; images from pasteboard versus file URLs; rejected drop; drop during edit |
| Paste into composer | Image, file list, RTF attachments, text-only; keyboard versus menu if known; unsupported input; ordinary paste must not trigger clipboard inspection beyond app behavior |
| Add into existing preview | Paste, drop and add control; preserve initial route; remove/reorder; mixed acquisition; remove all |
| Preview and edit | Crop/draw/edit, cancel editor, return to preview, replace media; file/media toggle, album/ungroup, spoiler, caption presence if useful; no caption content |
| Submit preview | Send control, Enter/Cmd-Enter, silent send, schedule, paid confirmation if applicable; cancel confirmation; repeat activation |
| Rich text media | Paste routes into rich editor, file panels, returning to composer, replace/edit existing message; distinguish edits from new sends |
| Voice/video recording | Press/release, locked recording, preview, discard, re-record, microphone/camera denial or interruption; never record audio samples |
| GIF/sticker/emoji | Picker, recent/favorite, suggestion, search, inline result; no search terms or item identifiers; distinguish reaction from message |
| Inline results and reusable content | Inline bot result, quick reply, resumed draft; no bot identity or command/query text; label only routes actually known |
| Forward/share inside app | Context menu, shortcut, selection toolbar, gallery; recipient-picker cancellation, target change, multi-target; no target IDs |
| System share extension | `TelegramShare/SESelectController.swift`; independent process/account selection and privacy resolution; no app singleton assumptions or screen-package dependencies |
| Other attachment kinds | Location, contact, poll, checklist and other actually supported items; own minimal flows only where useful; no coordinates or contact fields |
| Edit existing message | Paste/drop replacement, edit caption/media, cancel, local failure; separate editing task from fresh composition |
| Recover a failed send | User retry, change input, choose alternate path, cancel; distinguish local retry from automatic network retry |

Cross these with relevant ordinary peer kinds, Saved Messages, thread/topic, scheduled mode, multiple windows/accounts, account switch, logout, background/foreground, offline/reconnect, controller reuse and privacy transitions. Use pairwise coverage for ordinary combinations plus all high-risk boundary cases; do not claim an exhaustive Cartesian product was tested.

For each family, run secret-context versions, including an ordinary-looking child opened from a secret chat. Where an action is unavailable in secret chats, test the guard and direct producer path instead of inventing a UI scenario.

## 6. Subsequent scenario families

| Priority | Family | Questions and useful path distinctions |
|---|---|---|
| P1 | Chat discovery/navigation | Chat list, folder, archive, search, notification, deep link, peer profile, keyboard navigation; result selected versus abandoned, no query or link |
| P1 | Message actions | Reply/edit/forward/delete/react from menu, shortcut, double-click or selection toolbar where supported; confirmations, undo and cancellation |
| P1 | Search | Global versus within-chat; filters, pagination, no-results shown, result selection, exit; bucket result volume only if necessary |
| P1 | Media consumption | Open from chat/shared media/search, download explicitly, cancel/save/share, playback failure and recovery; no watched-content identity or playback fingerprint |
| P2 | Calls | Entry surface, permission prompt result, pre-connection abandonment, device selection/recovery; server already knows much of call lifecycle |
| P2 | Settings/privacy | Search versus navigation, control discovery, save/cancel, recoverable error; exclude passcode/password entry, recovery phrases and Passport data |
| P2 | Groups/channels | Creation, invitations, permissions, join-request setup, visibility and administration; reuse and improve existing flows |
| P2 | Stories | Entry to viewer/composer/reply, edit and abandon, local preparation errors; secret-derived paths excluded |
| P2 | Premium/Stars/gifts | Entry offer, product selection, confirmation/cancel, recovery; no balance, payment details or duplicate transaction reporting |
| P2 | Bots/mini apps | How opened and left, permission prompt outcome; no page URLs, bot IDs, mini-app internals or arbitrary script-provided data |
| Deferred | Authentication and highly sensitive surfaces | Schema 1 is authorized-account only. Login coverage needs a separate design; wallet recovery, credentials and identity documents remain excluded. |

## 7. What can help prevent automation abuse

Client analytics cannot establish that a human performed an action: modified clients can omit or forge it. Treat it as optional, untrusted supporting evidence. Enforcement must depend on independently evaluated server evidence; absence of analytics, missing preceding clicks, fast use, paste, bulk workflows or keyboard-only operation is never sufficient for restriction.

Candidate research questions, only after privacy and UX coverage are sound:

- Do bounded counts of repeated local submission rejection and recovery improve investigation beyond server-known request failures?
- Do aggregate route distributions or repeated journey patterns add measurable value beyond existing server rates, recipient patterns and abuse reports?
- Does a local UX issue produce behavior that currently resembles automation, such as repeated activation after weak feedback?

Use coarse per-session counts/rate buckets and short-lived aggregates, not raw input streams or individual behavioral fingerprints. Do not build a universal “human score,” collect accessibility status for classification, or claim that normal UI paths prove legitimacy. Do not instrument secret contexts even for abuse prevention.

Keep abuse research separate from UX reporting: document the purpose, permitted joins, access, retention and evaluation owner. Start in observation-only evaluation with no enforcement. Measure incremental benefit and false positives against legitimate keyboard, accessibility, professional/bulk and poor-network workflows. Account for sampling, offline loss, disabled analytics and client-version coverage; missing telemetry means unknown. Retention and access limits must be agreed before rollout, not invented by the client implementation.

## 8. Runtime and transport work

- Default-deny collection until an eligible account and explicit enable policy are installed. Keep rollout disabled through this project unless separately authorized.
- Bind recorder and sink to the initiating account and policy generation. Choose silent revocation on account switch for the initial implementation; never redirect delayed work to the new active account. Account identifiers remain local.
- Centralize production validation: name, allowed and required params, types, enum membership, flow-specific steps/params, menu/item pairing, finite and non-negative timings, count bounds, depth and serialized byte limit. Drop invalid data in release builds too. Diagnostic counters must not include rejected payloads or excluded-context activity.
- Preserve occurrence time independently from send time; use a monotonic clock for elapsed durations. Give summaries their documented finish occurrence time. Serialize ordering and admission so sequence numbers and queue order agree under concurrent callbacks.
- Ensure disable/revocation wins against simultaneous send, finish, deinit and queue dispatch without deadlocks or invoking external closures under a lock.
- Bound memory, snapshots, event size, queue count/bytes/age, and events per journey/session. Define drop policy and sampling in registry/runtime configuration. Sample whole journeys at eligible start so denominators and steps remain comparable; never sample around privacy rules.
- Add crash recovery only after privacy-safe snapshot ownership, versioning, expiry and purge exist. Persist only allowlisted ordinary flow state; quarantine/discard unknown older state. Emit interrupted once after authorized eligible recovery, never in another account or after opt-out. No snapshot for excluded or unresolved flows.
- Reuse the core transport instead of adding a second queue. Audit every core/direct caller and existing persisted schema before activation. Distinguish product analytics from functional protocol operations; record any necessary submodule changes explicitly.
- Avoid adding feature logic to TGUIKit or another hub. Keep policy/recording in AppAnalytics and route metadata at feature boundaries through existing hooks. Shared controller lifecycle APIs should carry only the minimum scope abstraction; never add screen-to-screen package dependencies.

## 9. Scenario ledger and trace contract

Add a separate machine-readable interaction ledger during implementation, alongside the existing screen inventory. Do not overload `coverage/macos.yaml` with claims its checker cannot verify.

Each scenario needs: stable local scenario ID; priority; product question; source file and handler; availability; entry/submit routes; scope owner; async handoffs; steps and terminal boundary; registry references; positive expected trace; secret/unresolved expected empty trace; test location; status (`planned`, `wired`, `tested`, `deferred`, `not_applicable`) with reason and evidence. IDs in this ledger are test metadata, not transmitted user identifiers.

Proposed example traces, pending registry approval:

- Attach → choose two photos → switch to files → submit with Cmd-Enter → local enqueue accepted: one summary retaining `entry_point=attach_menu`, file mode, attachment bucket and final submit method.
- Paste image → preview → cancel: one cancelled summary, no send outcome.
- Drop files → reject unsupported item → keep accepted items → add by paste → submit: one journey preserving drop entry, meaningful rejection/addition steps and one accepted outcome.
- Open picker → cancel before choosing: one cancellation at acquisition; do not omit it from the denominator.
- Submit → recoverable preparation failure → retry → accepted: one journey with a retry step, not two completed sends.
- Any equivalent secret-chat path, including editor close after returning to an ordinary chat: no analytics trace, debug payload, snapshot, queue entry or delayed emission.

## 10. Test and verification plan

Write tests around semantic boundaries, not one assertion per call site. Inject a fake clock, policy, account owner and capture sink/queue. Use fixture-driven expected traces so route preservation and duplicates are visible.

| Suite | Required checks |
|---|---|
| Privacy unit tests | Secret, unresolved and inherited excluded scopes; typed/raw APIs, flows, menus, lifecycle, standalone panels, debug hooks and legacy paths all produce zero collection/output |
| Privacy lifecycle tests | Enter/leave secret UI, multiple windows, hidden child, reusable chat controller, delayed picker, deinit, background, crash recovery, account switch/logout; no stale source, timing or replay |
| Concurrency tests | Barrier-controlled disable versus send/enqueue/flush; finish versus deinit; account replacement versus completion; terminal emission at most once and no deadlock |
| Journey fixtures | Attach/paste/drop equivalence where appropriate, distinct origin where required, mixed acquisition, cancel, reject/retry, scheduling, direct-send bypass, album splitting, editing and draft resume |
| Schema tests | Unknown names/params, wrong enums/types, invalid flow-step pairing, free-form error strings, oversized/negative/non-finite data rejected before debug/queue; generated artifacts match registry |
| Queue tests | Event occurrence time, batching, bounded offline growth, failure/drop policy, disable purge, legacy/unscoped entries, account isolation, snapshot expiry and one-time interrupted recovery |
| Manual integration | Real UI handlers for the route matrix, keyboard/menu and mouse operation, accessibility workflows, multiple windows/accounts, ordinary and secret chats; inspect captured traces and persisted queue |
| Runtime/performance | No added clipboard polling, synchronous disk work in input handlers or payload construction while disabled; bounded recorder memory; measure input/preview overhead against the disabled baseline |
| Build/regression | AppAnalytics tests plus affected feature builds and share extension where touched; existing flow fixtures; relevant send/edit/schedule regressions |

For privacy tests, an empty network capture alone is insufficient: inspect recorder admission, logs, snapshots and Postbox operations. In enabled test builds, use an isolated fake transport by default. Real test-account transport checks come only after exclusion tests pass. Do not transmit real user content for validation.

Track every manual scenario as passed, failed or unrun with build/revision and evidence. A source tag check cannot certify a working UI route. Do not say “everything tested” when a build or scenario could not run.

## 11. Implementation order and gates

1. **Privacy foundation:** default-deny scope, account ownership/revocation, no-op recorder, secret descendants, legacy audit and queue gate/purge. Acceptance: adversarial negative tests pass while rollout stays off.
2. **Schema and validation:** review the minimal media journey vocabulary, precise terminal meanings, coarse timing/count buckets, runtime validator and fixtures. Acceptance: generator/lint/compatibility checks pass and malformed payloads never reach a sink.
3. **Media vertical slice:** attach, paste and drop from entry through preview, editing, local acceptance, cancellation and retry. Acceptance: real route traces retain origins; one terminal result; all secret variants empty.
4. **Remaining composition and recovery:** recording, pickers, inline results, rich editor, forwarding, drafts, scheduling and share extension as available. Acceptance: ledger evidence and feature regressions for each implemented route.
5. **Broader journeys:** expand P1 then selected P2 scenarios based on product questions. Acceptance: a reviewed decision/use for every new metric, including denominators and exclusions.
6. **Operational hardening:** bounded persistence, crash recovery if justified, version/coverage metadata, sampling, access/retention decisions and reporting definitions. Acceptance: queue/privacy/concurrency/performance verification and declared untested gaps.
7. **Possible rollout, separate decision:** local capture → test accounts → small controlled cohort with kill switch → review data quality and overhead. Do not enable analytics or add abuse enforcement merely because instrumentation compiles.

## 12. Decisions to revisit during implementation

Defaults for this plan: application-wide suspension around secret surfaces; local-only journey tokens; one summary for a composition task; coarse timing for new paths; silent revocation on account change; no anti-bot enforcement; analytics stays off.

Before a production rollout, settle the analytics consent/opt-out behavior, server retention and access, exact queue/sampling budgets, event consumers and dashboard owners, and whether any server correlation beyond existing account transport is actually needed. These do not block writing and testing the disabled privacy foundation.

## 13. Planning verification

The initial audit was source-only. Documentation whitespace and local Markdown-link checks passed. Registry lint, generated-file checks, screen coverage with tags, and Python tooling tests were attempted but could not execute because PyYAML is missing from the available Python runtimes. Installing it into an isolated temporary environment also failed because pip could not obtain a distribution. This initially blocked those checks. A later network-enabled installation into the same isolated environment succeeded; current results are recorded in §14. The initial failure was not a reported test pass or an established code failure.

Rerun from the repository root using a Python environment with PyYAML:

```sh
python3 docs/analytics/tools/tg_events.py lint
python3 docs/analytics/tools/tg_events.py gen --check --swift packages/AppAnalytics/Sources/AppAnalytics/AnalyticsRegistry.swift --swift-coverage packages/AppAnalytics/Sources/AppAnalytics/AnalyticsCoverage.swift
python3 docs/analytics/tools/tg_events.py coverage --tags --report /tmp/telegram-analytics-coverage.md
python3 -m unittest discover -s docs/analytics/tools -p 'test_*.py'
```

Swift runtime and end-to-end UI tests were not run during the initial documentation pass. That pass changed no runtime code, registry, generated artifacts or enablement settings. Subsequent implementation verification is recorded below.

## 14. Implementation progress

### Scoped collection foundation

Implemented in `packages/AppAnalytics`:

- The hub defaults to disabled. Typed/raw send calls require an explicit eligible `AnalyticsScope`; missing scope denies collection, and event/data autoclosures are not evaluated when denied.
- Scope issuance requires the active account owner and an explicit ordinary classification. Excluded/unresolved contexts, other accounts and other hub instances cannot supply a valid scope. The peer-ID helper rejects secret and unsupported namespaces before peer loading.
- Disable, account replacement/logout and observed excluded surfaces permanently revoke existing scopes. Revocation clears registered flow state, visibility history and session state without terminal events. Re-enabling requires new scopes.
- A serial collection executor orders admission and revocation. Log callbacks are followed by a second eligibility check before sink admission. Already admitted sink work completes before a concurrent disable returns; downstream asynchronous Postbox/transport work has its own account-store grant and revocation checks, described below.
- Flows without eligible scope are inert. Flow origin is explicit rather than inferred from a global latest screen. `init(engine:scope:)` checks the scope against that engine's account. Raw sink construction is internal for conformance tests.
- Screen entries retain their scope; unscoped controllers/panels are ignored. Known secret screen IDs and peer tags trigger exclusion, even for otherwise unmapped controllers. Secret source/peer payloads are rejected as defense in depth.
- Existing name/param checks now reject invalid events before logging or sink submission in release builds too. Generated type/enum/flow validation is now implemented; see the schema-validation progress below.

Existing action events have not yet been migrated to explicit scopes and consequently remain denied, even if someone changes the startup switch. Main media handoffs now carry immutable privacy contexts, as recorded below. This is an intermediate safety boundary, not completed route coverage. Do not add automatic fallback scopes from the current screen to restore counts.

Remaining before the privacy foundation is complete: broader scope inheritance in controllers and hooks, private descendants after navigation changes, and real UI integration tests. Existing legacy UI call sites are denied at the core boundary; migrate useful ones to scoped, validated events or retire them. Continue the media route ledger and propagation described above. The media task registry/recorder is now drafted below; anti-bot enforcement has not been added.

Verification uses the real AppAnalytics package and its existing dependencies, not replacement runtime stubs. **31 tests passed in both debug and release for the scoped foundation; the transport integration also passes all 31 in debug**, including the original flow fixtures and 18 privacy/concurrency cases. Tests cover denied payload construction, wrong-owner/hub scopes, permanent revocation, state erasure, secret screens/payloads, late completion, multiple excluded surfaces, re-entrant revocation and concurrent terminal emission. Whitespace and documentation-link checks pass. Full app/UI tests remain unrun; these package results do not prove UI scope propagation. Real Postbox queue coverage is recorded below. The PyYAML blocker is now resolved; current registry and coverage results are recorded below.

```sh
CLANG_MODULE_CACHE_PATH=/tmp/telegram-analytics-module-cache SWIFTPM_MODULECACHE_OVERRIDE=/tmp/telegram-analytics-module-cache swift test --package-path packages/AppAnalytics --scratch-path /tmp/telegram-analytics-debug-verified --disable-sandbox --jobs 4
CLANG_MODULE_CACHE_PATH=/tmp/telegram-analytics-module-cache SWIFTPM_MODULECACHE_OVERRIDE=/tmp/telegram-analytics-module-cache swift test --package-path packages/AppAnalytics --scratch-path /tmp/telegram-analytics-build --disable-sandbox --jobs 4 -c release
```

### Persistent queue and legacy transport boundary

Implemented in TelegramCore and connected to the AppAnalytics hub:

- macOS denies unscoped `addAppLogEvent`, including the transaction overload. Legacy network statistics, network telemetry recording/reporting, split-test events and the direct small-log request are disabled on macOS. The network recorder gate precedes server configuration, Debug defaults and the internal recording override; the existing startup cleanup removes its old local store when recording is denied. Other platforms retain their legacy eligibility behavior.
- The hub lazily obtains an account-store grant for eligible events and invalidates it on policy revocation. A random grant identifier is persisted with queue entries as local metadata; it is never included in `inputAppEvent`. Scoped wire events always use `peer = 0`.
- Admission is rechecked inside the Postbox transaction and immediately before subscribing to the network request. A queued callback cannot regain eligibility after disable, account replacement, context destruction or later re-enable. Revocation cancels active requests, completes the waiting batch and schedules removal of invalid queue entries.
- Pending events are purged on every revocation, including secret-surface suspension. Startup removes legacy or prior-process grants without transmission. This is best-effort persistence within a live grant; cross-launch replay and unfinished-journey recovery are intentionally not implemented. Missing events are not evidence of abandonment or abuse.
- Scoped events are bounded to 16 KiB each, 512 queued events, 1 MiB total serialized payload bytes, and a 24-hour lifetime, with at most five minutes of future timestamp tolerance. Trimming drops oldest entries first. These byte bounds describe encoded operations, not total database overhead.
- Batches contain at most 50 events and 256 KiB of encoded operations. The first pending event starts a 30-second deadline; subsequent arrivals do not restart it. Explicit flush wakes a waiting batch. Scoped requests time out after 30 seconds or the remaining event lifetime, whichever is shorter, and failed/stalled batches are discarded.

Debug and release verification: **21 queue/privacy tests and 60 adjacent network-telemetry tests pass in each configuration**. Queue tests use real temporary Postbox databases and an injected network sender, covering legacy purge, wrong-account grants, delayed persistence, prepared batches, revocation/cancellation, timestamp and size limits, queue trimming, timeout, batching deadlines and flush. A regression test reproduced loss of a flush marker during the next enqueue; trimming now preserves authorized markers. The integrated AppAnalytics package also passes its 31 tests. These tests do not contact a server or exercise native UI. The empty-database test fixture emits existing MediaBox `t19` initialization warnings; assertions and teardown complete successfully.

### Interaction source audit

`coverage/macos-interactions.md` records 22 media routes and their exclusion/ownership matrix. Source inspection confirms Shift-drop bypasses preview, paste can open the rich editor or replace an existing message, and one preview submission can invoke several album batches plus a text send. These require a task-owned outcome and explicit acquisition/submit origin; the current callbacks do not carry either. This is audit evidence only, not tested interaction instrumentation.

### Generated runtime validation

`AnalyticsValidation.swift` validates against metadata emitted by `tg_events.py`, including supported macOS events/screens, enum/bucket values, booleans, finite nonnegative exact integers, menu/item and setting/value pairing, and the selected flow's own params and steps. Unknown free-form fields are no longer accepted by `flow.end`. Validation runs before debug logging and sink admission, including after common metadata is added. Rejected events do not consume a sequence number.

Flow setters validate before storing; invalid values or custom unregistered steps discard state silently. Unknown flow specs do not start a recorder. Timings cannot crash on non-finite values; backwards flow clocks discard the record. `setIfAbsent` does not construct unused fallback values. The existing flow fixtures still pass unchanged.

The registry now makes the existing flow-envelope requirements explicit (`steps`, `step_count`, `duration_ms`), narrows visibility-flow peers to its documented channel/group values, and encodes the documented success-only result as an `outcomes` condition. This tightens validation without adding interaction event names or changing successful fixture traces. Numeric, metadata and payload bounds are documented in IMPLEMENTING.md §7.

Verification: **43 AppAnalytics tests pass in both debug and release**, including 12 new adversarial validation tests; **18 Python tooling tests pass**; lint and generated-file checks pass. PyYAML 6.0.3 is installed only in `/tmp/telegram-analytics-plan-venv`. Both configurations use the real package and dependencies. Whitespace and local Markdown-link checks pass.

The now-runnable screen coverage check reports **23 baseline errors**: 13 unmapped surfaces and 10 missing-tag reports for `ChatAdditionController`. Source inspection confirms that subclass inherits its chat initialization from `ChatController`; the tag scanner currently inspects the subclass file alone. These need mapping/scanner work during UI integration, not duplicate runtime tags merely to silence a check. The report is `/tmp/telegram-analytics-coverage.md`. No UI scenario has been certified by these schema tests.

### Immutable UI privacy contexts

`AnalyticsContext` keeps a classification and the original account-bound scope. Restricting by another peer can only tighten eligibility; inheriting a revoked context never obtains a replacement scope. `AnalyticsPresentation` holds an independent exclusion lease through native-panel delays and callbacks. A panel with no explicit context is unresolved and excludes collection.

`ChatController` classifies the chat namespace immediately after initialization, before peer data arrives. The lifecycle tracker establishes exclusion at `viewWillAppear`. Main attachment handlers, composer file/image/RTF paste and drop-to-preview callbacks capture context before async work and pass it through `ChatUIHooks`, preview initialization and preview editors. Rich-editor entry points also restrict referenced source peers. Unscoped children presented during an excluded interval retain that exclusion after the parent closes. These changes propagate privacy, not interaction origin or outcomes.

The 12 context tests bring the privacy/schema suite to **55 passing tests in debug and release**. They use real TGUIKit controllers and cover early namespace exclusion, child lifetime, native-presentation leases, immutable inheritance, account changes, late callbacks, foreign hubs, source restrictions and ordinary positive controls. This does not prove native picker behavior or all navigation wrappers.

Known integration gaps at that checkpoint included generic modal lifecycle hooks, Stories preview-constructor context and direct Shift-drop. Those now have source fixes described below. Edit replacements, broader gallery/profile/forwarding descendants and multi-window native picker behavior remain unverified. Ordinary root chat contexts are classification-only; action capture, not a global visibility fallback, supplies eligible scopes.

A standalone PreviewSenderUI SwiftPM build could not resolve the nested RingBuffer package; the intended Xcode workspace provides a root-package override. The workspace verification build was stopped when the user chose to build locally. Their compiler feedback exposed one preview-view/controller context mix-up, which was fixed by capturing the controller context before the video-cover picker callback. Their linker feedback exposed callers compiled against older signatures; FilePanels and screen-tracker APIs now retain forwarding overloads with the same conservative privacy policy. App build verification remains with the user; no successful full app build is claimed here.

### Media task recorder and registry draft

The draft `chat.media_compose` flow and `AnalyticsMediaJourney` now implement a task-level contract for attach-file, photo/video attachment, image picker, clipboard, drop and explicitly unknown origins. Acquisition origin cannot be overwritten by later additions or submission method. The summary carries bucketed current attachment count, final mode, optional known invocation method, bounded retry count and local blocker, plus separate submit method/scheduling/silent fields. No IDs, paths, content or source-app metadata are accepted.

One task owns all local enqueue parts, including album chunks and extra text. An optional submission handle keeps it alive after preview destruction and deduplicates callbacks by bounded local positions. Every known part must finish before the terminal summary: all accepted yields success; partial or total rejection yields fail with a distinct result. Partial acceptance inside one part remains partial. Unknown results, unexpectedly destroyed tasks, invalid aggregation inputs and policy revocation discard silently. Explicit parent close/cancel is ignored once submission starts. Pre-submit blockers and child cancellation steps remain recoverable.

The registry now supports per-flow `timing_resolution_ms`; this flow emits only whole-second step/total durations and validation rejects finer values. Existing flow fixtures retain their timing contract. Storage is bounded to 50 steps, 256 submission parts and a capped retry bucket. Functional sending must never depend on whether analytics returns a handle. UI callback wiring is partial and per-route native tests are still pending; these changes alone cannot distinguish real users' routes in production. The registry remains a draft requiring review before shipping, and collection remains disabled.

Verification: **72 AppAnalytics tests pass in debug and release**, including 17 media-task tests. These cover entry/submit separation, mixed acquisitions, direct-send without preview, recoverable blockers, explicit versus unobserved closure, partial acceptance within and across batches, duplicate/out-of-order/concurrent callbacks, retained submission lifetime, zero disabled/private state, revocation while results are pending, invalid/bounded input, step limits and coarse timing. **19 Python tests**, registry lint, generated-file checks, whitespace and local Markdown links pass. The new tooling case rejects invalid timing resolutions. Original flow fixtures pass unchanged. Logs: `/tmp/telegram-analytics-media-tests.log` and `/tmp/telegram-analytics-media-release-tests.log`. These are recorder/schema tests; app/native route verification has not been substituted by them.

Next integration boundary: preserve existing public callback signatures when adding observed-send paths. `PreviewSenderController.sendCurrentMedia` calls `sendMessage` before media batches; that can be a no-op or send a rich draft/other composer state. Determine actual operations before counting parts. `Sender.enqueue(media:caption:...)` already returns the combined IDs for its media and `additionText`; do not count that internal extra text twice. Inspect custom-chat enqueue semantics separately before interpreting nil or empty results. Keep each part's result local and pass only its bounded classification to the recorder.

### Coverage-tool corrections during app verification

`coverage --tags` now accepts an explicit `tags_from` reference to an inventoried, unambiguous declared base class, with the same mapped screen set (or a subset). It verifies the tags in the actual source chain and rejects missing/unrelated bases, ambiguity and cycles. Commented code and string examples no longer satisfy tag checks. Eight regression tests cover these cases; all **27 tooling tests pass**. `ChatAdditionController` declares its inherited tags from `ChatController`, whose initializer it calls directly, resolving the ten false positives without duplicating runtime tags.

Source inspection also confirms `SearchTabsController` and `EntertainmentSidebarController` are containers for already mapped child screens, and `NewsListController` is embedded table content. Their inventory decisions now record these reasons. This does not authorize collection from their children or certify lifecycle propagation.

Ten real inventory gaps remain and intentionally keep the coverage gate red: `EditImageCutoutController`, `EditVideoController`, `EditVideoCropController`, `EditVideoToolsController`, `NewsController`, `QrStudioController`, `WalletPeerBalanceController`, `StickerPackTitleController`, `giftTransferConfirmController` and `showGiftTransfer`. They need reviewed screen vocabulary and appropriate tagging, not an ignore entry. `EditVideoController` explicitly presents its crop/tools children as modals; sticker title and gift-transfer functions create purposeful InputData screens. Update these during their feature integration and regenerate the report only when the complete check passes.

Media UI integration has begun with a journey factory on `ChatInteraction`, a companion preview callback and a preview-owned task slot/lifecycle handling. These are now connected to entry handlers and observed-send companions; see the integration sections below. A newly introduced but unused hosting protocol was removed after the user's compiler feedback; do not restore a cross-module protocol solely for this handoff. Existing preview callback signatures remain intact. App build verification remains with the user.

### Acquisition-to-preview wiring

Main attach-file/photo-video/image-picker handlers, file/image/RTF paste and media/file drop-to-preview handlers now create one scoped task and retain it through preparation. The creation helper rejects edit state, custom chat modes and absent/invalid scopes. Drop task creation occurs only on accepted drop; text-only paste creates no media task. Invocation is left unknown unless the handler proves it. Native file cancellation, all/partial selection rejection, missing files, slow mode and empty RTF preparation use explicit local boundaries. Ambiguous image-picker nil output is discarded.

An additive preview callback carries the task beside the original privacy context, and an additive `ChatUIHooks.attachPreviewJourney` hook binds the concrete preview without changing existing initializer/callback signatures. Preview appearance records its step and explicit dismissal cancels only before `sent`. The full handoff and limitations are recorded in `coverage/macos-interactions.md`. Swift frontend parse and whitespace checks pass for all seven edited feature/bridge files; full type checking and app verification remain with the user. The previously passing 72 recorder tests are not new UI test evidence.

### Local submission aggregation wiring

The preview now registers one composer part plus every album batch before invoking observed-send callbacks. Send-control, Enter, Command-Enter and send-menu origin survive scheduling callbacks. The final count/mode and scheduling/silent flags are captured at submission. `sent` still controls functional dismissal; it is never acceptance evidence.

Additive `ChatInteraction` companions preserve existing public callback signatures. Actual rich-draft, plain-text, forward and media enqueue results resolve bounded parts. The composer groups text and forwarding (at most two results), while an empty composer explicitly skips its part. Media enqueue results already include their additional text. Missing forwarding inputs or unknown/no callback results discard silently. Group callbacks retain the task after modal destruction; duplicate and late callbacks cannot revive it. Custom-chat result semantics remain excluded, and captured privacy contexts restrict forwarding source namespaces.

Eight additional recorder tests exercise no-op versus unknown results, grouped partial outcomes, group destruction, secret/disable/account revocation, invalid positions before lazy classification, duplicates and concurrency. **80 AppAnalytics tests pass in both debug and release**, including 25 media-task tests; **27 Python tooling tests**, registry lint and generated-file checks pass. Logs remain `/tmp/telegram-analytics-media-tests.log` and `/tmp/telegram-analytics-media-release-tests.log`. Feature files pass Swift frontend parsing and whitespace checks; this is not feature type checking or native callback verification. App build verification remains with the user.

Next: preview additions/edits, recoverable blockers and scheduling/options, followed by actual eligible and excluded route verification. The subsequent checkpoint below covers generic modal lifecycle, the Stories preview handoff and direct Shift-drop. Broader private-source navigation remains a privacy blocker. Keep collection disabled and do not interpret the current partial terminal summaries as a completion-rate or anti-bot denominator.


### Modal exclusion, Stories preview construction and direct drop

`ModalController` now invokes the neutral framework lifecycle hooks for its own identity. Its will/did-appear hooks precede forwarding to the navigation child; its did-disappear hook follows child callbacks. This establishes exclusion before child work and avoids a collection gap during dismissal. The wrapper still delegates functional responder/navigation behavior as before. Four regression tests exercise the real modal/navigation lifecycle with window lookup replaced by nil: exclusion before child callbacks, a dependent child outliving its parent, dismissal before did-appear, retention through descendant dismissal callbacks and an ordinary positive control. Ordinary wrapper eligibility does not grant a scope to its children.

The generic Stories preview factory was declared but not installed. The app now installs it and an additive context-aware companion. Stories uses the companion so the immutable captured context reaches `PreviewSenderController` during initialization, including its secondary interaction and emoji child. Its attach-file/photo-video handlers capture the actual story peer classification before the native panel; missing peer data is unresolved. The same context passes through the panel and preview callback. Shared chat action capture also tightens eligibility using the loaded presentation peer, including hosts whose chat location is a placeholder. The old factory signature remains available. Stories send outcomes and all other Stories descendants remain unverified.

M08 Shift-drop now starts a file/drop task only in the accepted handler and passes it through an additive `sendMediaWithJourney` companion. Existing voice/other callers use the original callback without a media-composition task. Direct send registers one aggregate result for the actual container enqueue, preserving direct invocation and scheduled mode without a preview step. This enqueue produces one message per container; a result-count mismatch is unknown and discards the summary, while nil IDs preserve partial/total rejection. Missing completion, unsupported/custom modes and policy revocation cannot claim success. The later dialog checkpoint below adds explicit date-selector cancellation; other unobserved closure still discards.

Verification checkpoint: **84 AppAnalytics tests pass in debug and release**, including 16 context tests and the four new modal cases. The first dependency build was interrupted by concurrent source edits, and the next attempt used a stale SwiftPM source list. Replanning with `--disable-build-manifest-caching` included the new dependency sources and the tests passed. Logs: `/tmp/telegram-analytics-modal-full-tests.log` and `/tmp/telegram-analytics-modal-release-tests.log`. Feature-source parsing and whitespace checks remain separate from full app type checking/native route execution, which stays with the user. Collection remains disabled.


### Preview mutations and option selection

M15/M16 now pass an explicit paste/drop step into the existing addition callback. The step and updated count/mode are recorded only if the normalized URL list actually changes. Text-only paste, duplicate additions and empty results do not invent an acquisition. Removal updates the bucket after an actual deletion, including zero; reordering records only a changed order. These use the existing UI state and do not add filesystem/clipboard reads for analytics.

Explicit media/file and grouped/ungrouped menu choices add a step only when they change the current state. Spoiler toggle records the action. These steps remain on the original task and cannot replace its entry route; the recorder ignores mutations after submission or revocation. Archive selection, add-picker variants, editor outcomes, preparation/permission/caption blockers and retry attribution still need explicit coverage. No native preview route has been certified by syntax parsing or the recorder tests.


### Preview recovery and known editor outcomes

The recorder now separates acquisition blockers from submission blockers. `submissionBlocked` arms one bounded pending-retry flag; `retrySubmissionIfBlocked` consumes it only at the next invocation of the preview's send action. Repeated blocker callbacks cannot multiply the next retry. An initial size-filter rejection does not label the first send as a retry. The flag is cleared on submission, terminal outcome and every privacy/account/disable revocation. No new payload fields or fine-grained timing were added.

The preview marks existing return paths for caption limits, unfinished media/archive preparation, slow mode and permission denial as recoverable submission blockers. It does not change their functional behavior or log caption lengths, paths, permissions strings or preparation contents. Retry attribution remains on the original acquisition task even when the user adds or removes media before retrying.

Main image, paint and video editor openings now add an editor step. Image/video results add `apply_editor` only where the parent accepts the returned result; the paint editor emits its accepted drawing result before closing, which adds the step without treating its always-run close handler as acceptance. This step means the editor result was accepted, not that later rendering/upload succeeded. Optional neutral cancellation callbacks report the explicit image cancel button or a confirmed paint/video discard. They are consumed once and do not report cancellation after the editor has begun accepting a result. Generic close/deallocation is not interpreted as cancellation. Video Done with no changes emits no result, and unobserved image Escape/window dismissal remains unclassified; these are still ledger gaps.

Nested video crop/tools/paint and image paint/cutout controllers receive the original privacy context explicitly before presentation. The asynchronous image-to-paint handoff captures it before subscribing, and missing context is unresolved. ImageCanvasUI and AvatarEditorUI declare AppAnalytics only because these sources now use its context API. This does not certify all editor descendants or native presentation transitions.

Verification: **89 AppAnalytics tests pass in debug and release**, including five new retry/frozen-submission tests. They cover selection-versus-send blockers, duplicate retry notifications, recovery across preview changes, secret/disable/account revocation, post-submit mutations and bounded retry storage. Logs: `/tmp/telegram-analytics-recovery-tests.log` and `/tmp/telegram-analytics-recovery-release-tests.log`. Modified editor/preview Swift files pass frontend parsing and whitespace checks. Feature type checking and native callback/exclusion-matrix execution remain with the user's app verification. Collection remains disabled.

Next: explicit scheduling/paid/discard child outcomes, the remaining editor close paths, archive/add-picker vocabulary, screen inventory gaps, and the wider interaction families in §§5–9. Media progress does not cover recording, search, drafts, forwarding, settings or other app journeys; no claim of complete anti-abuse evidence or rollout readiness is made.


### Scheduling and confirmation outcomes

The date selector now exposes a neutral cancellation callback for its header close button and Escape. It consumes that callback once and clears it before date/when-online acceptance. Other generic programmatic/window dismissal is not reclassified as explicit cancellation. An additive context-aware navigation hook carries the original context into the selector and its calendar child. The original date-selector hook remains available; a host without the new hook retains the context through the legacy callback lifetime and does not invent a cancellation result.

Preview scheduling from the send action or send menu records open/cancel child steps. Cancelling returns to the same task; selecting a date continues with the original submit method. Direct Shift-drop carries its original captured context independently of its optional journey, so a revoked/denied task cannot freshen context when opening the selector. Cancelling that selector terminates the direct attempt, since no preview remains to resume.

Preview paid-confirmation and discard-confirmation openings now record child steps and hold a context presentation lease through dialog lifetime. Their explicit Cancel buttons add a child cancellation step; they do not abandon the preview. Confirming discard terminates the parent once. The existing alert API does not report Escape/window dismissal through its cancel handler; those paths stay unknown. Confirming payment with insufficient balance opens the existing purchase flow without inventing send acceptance. No dates, timestamps, prices, balances or checkbox values were added to telemetry.

Four recorder tests cover child cancellation followed by scheduled success, returning from discard before confirmed cancellation, direct-drop cancellation without a preview/submit step, and secret entry followed by late callbacks. **93 AppAnalytics tests pass in debug and release**. Logs: `/tmp/telegram-analytics-dialog-tests.log` and `/tmp/telegram-analytics-dialog-release-tests.log`. Feature source passes frontend parsing and whitespace checks. These tests assert the task contract, not native date-selector/alert behavior or feature type checking. The remaining native matrix needs an isolated capture sink and functional app verification; production analytics stays disabled.

Native cases still to execute: preview schedule Cancel/header close/Escape followed by send; paid Cancel followed by send; discard Cancel followed by confirmed discard; direct-drop schedule cancel versus accept; select date/when-online followed by late close; secret entry/account switch while each child is open; and parent destruction with a child/calendar still alive. Assert one terminal summary only where observed, no scope refresh after revocation, zero excluded state/output, and unchanged functional sending.


### Archive choice and editor inventory

The draft media task now has `media_mode=archive` and `select_archive`. The preview maps its actual sending state to media/file/archive for additions, deletion and final submission, rather than collapsing archive into file. Its original acquisition route is unchanged. `attachment_count` remains the number of selected input attachments, not the number of generated archive files or queued messages. Preparation blockers and retries remain on the same task. Tests cover archive acceptance after retry and returning through file/media modes before cancellation.

The registry now maps four previously uncovered, purposefully opened editor screens: `media_editor.video`, `media_editor.video.crop`, `media_editor.video.tools`, and `media_editor.image.cutout`. These are macOS entries and distinguish the main editor from its actual child modals. The cutout controller belongs to ImageCanvasUI; the coverage verifier caught and corrected an initial package mapping error. Generated class mapping supplies these single-screen identities, so no duplicate runtime tags were added. Mapping does not authorize collection: the existing scope/context gate and private descendant rules still apply.

**95 AppAnalytics tests pass in debug and release**, and all **27 tooling tests** pass. Registry lint (432 screens), generated-file checks, frontend parsing and whitespace checks pass. Logs: `/tmp/telegram-analytics-archive-tests.log`, `/tmp/telegram-analytics-archive-release-tests.log`. Native archive conversion/sending and editor transitions remain unverified.

`coverage --tags` now reports six genuine unresolved inventory entries, down from ten: NewsController, QrStudioController, WalletPeerBalanceController, StickerPackTitleController, giftTransferConfirmController and showGiftTransfer. The diagnostic log is `/tmp/telegram-analytics-current-coverage.log`; a successful full coverage report is not claimed. These need reviewed vocabulary and source/descendant privacy audits, especially peer QR/wallet surfaces and gift-recipient dialogs. They were not ignored to make the gate pass. Collection remains disabled.


### Recording privacy and route audit

Recording now has a dedicated R01–R14 source ledger in [macos-interactions.md](coverage/macos-interactions.md#recording-source-audit-and-task-contract). It distinguishes hold/locked entry, permissions, payment, pause/review/trim/resume, one-time media, explicit versus automatic sending, Stories and recording-to-transcription. Normal mode-switch clicks are not starts. Accessibility is not an abuse label. This is the design for the next task family, not a claim that recording events are implemented.

Privacy context now follows all four known recording constructors, recorder windows, permission signals, delayed data results, main-chat scheduling, recording confirmations and main-chat purchase controllers. The shared signal wrapper establishes exclusion on subscription and holds it through downstream callbacks and source cleanup. Main-chat media results carry their original context independently of any optional journey. Stories' audio result signal is protected, but the downstream Stories paid/purchase/enqueue handoff is still incomplete.

The current source also contains a recording-to-transcription route. It now receives the recorder's original context, retains exclusion through its independent active lifetime and view-removal animation, and transfers it into speech permission and error dialogs. It records no transcript, language or audio data. Future analytics must treat this as a branch to a text task, not cancelled or successfully sent media.

**102 AppAnalytics tests pass in debug and release**, including seven new signal-lifetime tests. Logs: `/tmp/telegram-analytics-recording-privacy-tests.log` and `/tmp/telegram-analytics-recording-privacy-release-tests.log`. Source parsing is separate from package type checking and native verification. No full app build was run; the user's build verification remains authoritative. Collection stays disabled, no recording vocabulary has been activated, and no bot classification/enforcement has been added. Next: complete Stories downstream context, define/test the bounded recording task, and wire known action/outcome boundaries against R01–R14. The other media gaps, screen inventory and wider P1/P2 families remain part of the goal.


### Stories recording handoff follow-up

Stories' recording results now carry original context into its direct-send companion, paid confirmation, context-aware purchase hook and delayed enqueue. Destination restriction can tighten but cannot renew the original scope. Main-chat recording purchases use the same hook, which holds exclusion from before purchase-controller construction through destruction, including asynchronous readiness. The old purchase callback remains available. All hooks have one verified app installer; no new feature-to-feature dependency was added.

The main-chat recording kind is now frozen at invocation and reused for eligibility, system permissions and eventual recorder construction. Previously it could be reread after an asynchronous prompt. The recording ledger includes an explicit attempt/recording/review/transcribing/submitting/terminal contract; it distinguishes draft insertion from text/media enqueue. Recording vocabulary and outcome wiring remain upcoming work, and native scenarios remain unrun.


Verification: **105 AppAnalytics tests pass in debug and release**, including three new destination-restriction cases. Logs: `/tmp/telegram-analytics-story-context-tests.log` and `/tmp/telegram-analytics-story-context-release-tests.log`. Modified feature sources pass frontend parsing and whitespace checks. Missing original context is now unresolved at Story direct-send and main-chat scheduling handoffs, rather than granting fresh eligibility at a delayed callback. No full app build/native trace is claimed; analytics remains disabled. Recording task instrumentation, the native exclusion matrix, remaining media routes, six inventory gaps and the wider interaction families are still outstanding.


### Dedicated recording journey model

The draft macOS `chat.recording` flow and `AnalyticsRecordingJourney` now implement the recording state contract. Typed results separate media enqueue, text enqueue and insertion into a draft. Permission callbacks, lock/review/trim/resume, one-time changes, child dialogs, transcription and submission have explicit allowed states. Submission is frozen and its result is lazy, deduplicated and rechecked after callback execution; missing output is unknown rather than success/cancellation. Privacy revocation erases the recorder. No functional recording UI uses this task yet, and production collection is still disabled.

Fourteen model tests exercise meaningful recovery paths and adversarial transitions, including denied initialization, reentrant revocation and a thousand review cycles with bounded state. The registry has 6 flows and passes lint/generated-file checks; all 27 tooling tests pass. Next is feature wiring against R01–R14, especially actual start status, recorder-to-transcription continuity and observed local send results. Do not equate a call to `start()`, view removal or signal completion with the corresponding user outcome.


Verification: **119 AppAnalytics tests pass in debug and release**, including all 14 recording-model tests. Logs: `/tmp/telegram-analytics-recording-model-tests.log` and `/tmp/telegram-analytics-recording-model-release-tests.log`. All 27 tooling tests, registry lint and generated-file consistency checks pass. This verifies the recorder model, not native route wiring; no full app build or UI verification was run. The broader goal remains in progress.


### Recording action integration

Main-chat and Story recorder states now retain the dedicated journey. Main entry preserves known long-press/Cmd-R activation through an additive callback without changing the legacy callback signature; accessibility activation remains unclassified. Existing return paths supply known permission/slow-mode failures. Video uses actual pipeline state, and audio uses its existing status callback with a positive recorded-duration check before marking start. Lock, review readiness, committed trim, resumed recording, one-time option changes and explicit cancellation are connected. Child confirmation cancellation after start keeps the parent journey active.

Transcription now retains the same journey when audio cleanup happens. It can report observed draft insertion, explicit cancellation and known preparation/permission failure. Generic dismissal is unknown. Media enqueue and transcription text-send outcome handoffs are still pending; no success is inferred from a stop/close or the existing fire-and-forget send callback. Partial cancellation/failure/draft coverage must not be presented as a complete recording funnel.

Trim comparisons are lazy inside the scope gate; only a boolean pending-change flag survives intermediate drag updates. New tests cover one committed step across many drag updates, no-op commits, resume clearing pending state, private revocation preventing a late comparison, and speech denial on the recording-to-text branch. Native app build/route verification remains with the user; the next implementation work is observed submission and result propagation.


Action-integration checkpoint: **122 AppAnalytics tests pass in debug and release**. Logs: `/tmp/telegram-analytics-recording-actions-tests.log` and `/tmp/telegram-analytics-recording-actions-release-tests.log`. ### Recorded-media submission handoff

Recording now has an explicit preparation request that freezes the task before conversion while allowing scheduling/payment confirmation to finish or cancel. The request is carried through additive recorder/ChatInteraction companions into the existing main-chat and Story enqueue paths. Success/rejection comes from the corresponding returned local result, with one-container/one-result validation inside the scope gate. Unknown/missing/mismatched results cannot fabricate success or cancellation. Existing functional sends do not depend on an analytics handle.

Release, Return and automatic video duration limit preserve their submit method. Automatic submission has a repeated-tick guard and retains its delivery callback independently of the closing recorder. Story release also hands off independently of overlay lifetime. Video result streams complete without a value on terminal stop/failure, preventing indefinite waiting and exclusion leases after discarded short recordings. These functional lifetime adjustments still require native app verification, including explicit-send/automatic-limit races.

The shared direct-media paths no longer attempt their old unscoped message-send/reply events. They report the useful client journey through observed results instead. Transcription's text-send path remains to be connected; the full native exclusion matrix, remaining media/inventory work and broader families are still outstanding. The ledger separates source integration from native proof.

### Sticker journey contract and model

The draft `stickers.edit` flow now separates five initiating routes: composer creation, adding a new image to a pack, adding an existing sticker, replacing a sticker and renaming a pack. Final destination can be send, favorites, a new/existing pack, replacement or rename. Image mode is only cutout/whole-image; no content, titles, emoji, identifiers or entered values are accepted by the model. Initial route remains fixed when a user cancels new-pack naming and chooses another destination.

`AnalyticsStickerJourney` requires observed acquisition/preparation and name acceptance before a submission. An explicit child naming cancellation returns composer creation to destination selection; cancelling a rename ends that task. Validation blockers stay recoverable. Observed submission rejection can be recoverable or terminal according to the actual UI behavior; retry count increases only on a subsequent submission after a retryable rejection. Every submission has a local attempt handle, so an old result or old handle's cleanup cannot complete/discard a later attempt. Result classification runs lazily within the original scope and is checked again after callbacks. Missing results, generic teardown and revoked context discard without a summary. State is bounded to 50 retained steps and 256 submission attempts, with whole-second timing and bucketed retries.

Twelve model tests cover the routes, destination changes, naming validation, whole-image fallback, retry identity, stale cleanup, duplicate/lost/reentrant results, disabled/private/unknown origins, account changes and bounds. The registry has seven flows; all 27 tooling tests and generated-file checks pass. The model is not yet wired into sticker UI. Integration must distinguish explicit Cancel controls from the cutout editor's reused `cancel()` close routine: the done-menu success closure also calls that routine. It must verify each engine API's success/error contract before resolving an attempt, and continue functional operations with no analytics handle. Ordinary origin admission, editor event callbacks, observed Send Sticker enqueue, native verification and the wider task remain unfinished.

This protects detached wallet observers that stop/dispose themselves before showing result UI, and applies to existing media/recording/gift signal integrations too. It does not extend protection to arbitrary upstream `afterCompleted` effects after delivery has returned; those still need their own explicit original-context handoff.

`PendingUpdateMessageManager.add` now has an additive per-attempt completion overload. The existing signature stays available. The chat's normal Save path holds its original exclusion lease until the detached result callback, including supersession and destruction. ### Media replacement journey and session ownership

`chat.media_edit` now describes media replacement separately from new-message composition. The first route is immutable; later paste/drop/picker/editor/attachment choices and accepted local replacements form a bounded history. Media/file mode follows the last applied replacement, so cancelling a different-mode picker cannot mislabel the draft. There are at most 256 acquisition attempts, 50 stored steps and whole-second timings. No identifiers, paths, text, content metadata or exact file sizes enter this journey.

ChatInteraction owns acquisition identity independently of analytics admission. Paste, drop, replacement picker, current-photo editor and text-only attachment conversion use the same guard; edit-session/location/mode changes and submission invalidate old callbacks. This also prevents an older conversion from changing a newer draft. Captured context is revalidated on every acquisition, and exclusion/revocation cannot start a fresh scope inside an existing journey. Generic cleanup discards; accessory dismissal and Escape invoke a distinct user-initiated cancellation path. Delayed discard confirmation checks its original session and privacy context.

Submission transfers journey ownership to the pending edit or scheduled request. The new ordinary-edit result contract and scheduled `.done(true)` establish applied success; known request errors establish failure. Unknown, cancelled, superseded, interrupted and false/no-result completion discard silently. Optimistic state and conversion completion never count as submission success. A save with an unresolved acquisition discards observation rather than attributing the old draft to the pending conversion. Existing public cancellation API remains available and defaults to non-user cleanup.
