### Scheduled media-edit recovery and stale callback regression

Scheduled failures now retain the original replacement journey while the editing owner is alive. Explicit upload stop records `cancel_submission` and returns to editing; a replacement started during upload records `supersede_submission`. The next actual submission advances a bucketed retry count once, including after repeated caption blockers. Ordinary detached failures remain terminal. Unknown disposal and unconfirmed completion discard silently. Each submitted handle carries its attempt number; stale results and destruction of old handles cannot resolve or discard a retry. Acquisition and submission attempts are independently capped at 256, with the existing 50-step and whole-second bounds.

ChatInteraction keeps the scheduled journey through retry and owns a separate functional submission identity. Choosing new media stops the old upload, clears loading and invalidates its callbacks independently of analytics admission. The scheduled progress, error and completion callbacks require the current identity as well as the message ID, preventing an older upload from mutating or clearing a newer draft for the same message. Explicit upload cancellation and edit-session/location/mode changes also invalidate that identity. IDs remain in-memory functional state and are never included in analytics.

All 27 tooling tests, generated consistency and 485 inventory decisions pass (`/tmp/telegram-analytics-media-edit-retry-coverage.md`). ### Explicit editor exits, unknown results and reentrant closure

The current-photo editing hook now has an additive event companion distinguishing explicit Cancel from unknown dismissal. It resolves once through an atomic gate, marks output before invoking consumers, and retains the captured context through constructor/callback/signal lifetimes. The installed factory reports generic close/destruction and constructor failure; old hook signatures remain available. Constructor failure completes without an output instead of leaving a never-ending result. Legacy empty completion remains unknown.

Image Cancel and Escape before commit now share one cancellation path. Close is idempotent and blocks late confirmation; commit state is set before publishing output to prevent a reentrant consumer from relabeling accepted output as cancellation. Both cancel and close hold their original context through callbacks and result cleanup. Closure during committed rendering without delivered output remains unknown. The media-edit model records `cancel_editor`, retains the previously applied replacement and mode, and rejects stale editor callbacks. Unknown current-editor exits discard observation.

**180 analytics tests pass in debug and release**, including three new editor cancellation/staleness/unknown cases (`/tmp/telegram-analytics-editor-exit-tests.log`, `/tmp/telegram-analytics-editor-exit-release-tests.log`). **Eight extracted editor/hook lifecycle tests pass** (`/tmp/telegram-analytics-image-editor-lifecycle-tests.log`); the fixture initially used an unsupported Promise initializer, corrected to the production default initializer plus `set`. The tests execute production close/cancel/confirmation, hook and signal-wrapper bodies against stand-ins. An in-memory negative variant restoring the old confirmation guard/order fails three regression cases; repository source was unchanged for that run (`/tmp/telegram-analytics-image-editor-without-confirm-guards.log`).

All 27 tooling tests and generated consistency checks pass. Changed feature sources parse and targeted whitespace checks pass. These checks do not establish native window behavior, full feature type checking, real image rendering, secret-origin account transitions or committed-render races. Those cases, early acquisition blockers, submit-control/date-dialog provenance and broader interaction families remain open. Analytics remains disabled; the full goal remains active.


### Acquisition rejection and recovery without replacing the valid draft

Single-file/image paste rejection while editing existing media now retains the acquisition route and observed size/file-availability/permission gate. File inspection failure is named `file_unavailable`; the code does not prove deletion or distinguish all filesystem failures. This observation path does not create a functional replacement token, stop a valid upload or invalidate an earlier conversion. Submitted journeys remain frozen, so invalid side attempts during an active submission are not folded into its result. A separate overlapping-task contract remains necessary for those cases.

Known empty picker selection and photo representation/link-preparation failures now resolve only their matching pending acquisition. The prior draft, applied mode and replacement count remain intact. Unknown picker disappearance still discards, and explicit cancellation stays separate. Failure/cancellation/acceptance from an old token cannot change the current acquisition. Rejected attempts consume the bounded acquisition budget, and the next actual submission records one recovery attempt. No paths, exact sizes or media content are added.

**186 analytics tests pass in debug and release**, including six new blocker/recovery/staleness/bounds cases (`/tmp/telegram-analytics-edit-acquisition-tests.log`, `/tmp/telegram-analytics-edit-acquisition-release-tests.log`). All 27 tooling tests, generated consistency and 485 inventory decisions pass (`/tmp/telegram-analytics-edit-acquisition-tool-tests.log`, `/tmp/telegram-analytics-edit-acquisition-coverage.md`). Modified feature sources parse and targeted whitespace checks pass. These are shared-model and source checks, not native pasteboard/picker/resource execution or full feature type checking.

Remaining acquisition work includes multi-file filtering, rejected drops before deliberate target invocation, unreadable-editor construction, overlapping side tasks and native privacy/account transitions. Date-dialog and submit-control provenance and the other interaction families also remain open. Analytics remains disabled; the full goal remains unfinished.


### Scheduled-message metadata is separate from editor retry ownership

Source inspection corrects the earlier checkpoints' overly broad “scheduled edit” wording: normal inline Save, even on an existing scheduled message, passes no explicit date and uses the detached manager. Only the explicit-date direct-request branch retains the editor. No normal UI route into that editing branch has yet been demonstrated. The current scenario ledger now describes this distinction; historical checkpoints above must not be treated as proof of native scheduled retry behavior.

`chat.media_edit.scheduled` now reflects either an explicit date or the existing message's scheduled namespace. Retry eligibility is a separate model argument, supplied by the chat from whether the editor remains active after failure. Detached scheduled failures stay terminal; upload cancellation cannot make them retryable. The original public one-argument model method remains available with its prior behavior. **189 analytics tests pass in debug and release**, including three new cases separating scheduling metadata from retry lifetime (`/tmp/telegram-analytics-edit-scheduling-semantics-tests.log`, `/tmp/telegram-analytics-edit-scheduling-semantics-release-tests.log`). Feature sources parse; native editing and the full app build remain unverified.


### Date-selector provenance and inline-result schedule forwarding

The legacy ChatUI date helper now binds the captured context before presentation, retains it during factory construction and establishes independent leases for selection and date actions. Unknown provenance defaults to unresolved. All current helper callers supply their origin, captured before delayed dispatch or confirmation: plain/rich drafts, media, inline results, commands, send-menu scheduling and suggested-post approval. Suggested posts restrict by the source message peer as well. The public factory signature is unchanged. This protects privacy through callbacks, but does not yet guard the functional draft/reply/destination snapshot.

The audit found an existing inline-result scheduling bug: the selected date was reflected in the analytics label but omitted from the enqueue call. The call now passes `scheduleTime`; the inspected core builder uses it to attach outgoing scheduling metadata. No network send was executed. Inline recurrence remains unsupported by that API and must not be inferred from date-dialog selection.

**Eight extracted-source tests pass** (`/tmp/telegram-analytics-chat-date-context-tests.log`): five exercise context construction, delayed/reentrant callbacks, unresolved fallback and argument forwarding; three execute the inline apply body for scheduled/immediate/rejected results. These compile production method bodies with context/UI/engine stand-ins, not native windows or the real policy. A negative in-memory variant removing date callback leases fails four cases with six assertions (`/tmp/telegram-analytics-chat-date-without-callback-context.log`). A second in-memory variant omitting `scheduleTime` fails both dated-send assertions while all five context tests and the immediate-send case pass (`/tmp/telegram-analytics-inline-without-schedule-time.log`). Neither negative run edits repository source. Changed feature sources pass frontend parsing and targeted whitespace checks. Analytics remains disabled.

The broader interaction audit and full goal remain unfinished. The user retains full app build verification.


### Delayed-send identity, duplicate callbacks and destination consistency

Scheduling now carries a functional one-use action in addition to analytics provenance. ChatInteraction changes its revision when send-relevant state changes; reverting a draft or location does not revive an earlier action. The comparison covers text/formatting, edit state, reply/thread, forwards and visibility, sender/destination/mode, suggested-post/effect/preview choices, price and slow-mode eligibility. It ignores unrelated presence, scroll and ordinary cursor updates. Rich-draft mutation invalidates locally and delivery compares the current shared draft. View disappearance, detachment and cleanup explicitly invalidate outstanding actions, including dialogs that retain the interaction.

The plain/rich draft, media, inline-result, command and send-menu date callbacks validate this action. Stale callbacks discard a media observation without inventing failure or cancellation. Consuming occurs before validation, preventing reentrant sends; duplicates do not run the invalidation callback and therefore cannot discard an accepted observation. Plain and inline paid-confirmation paths also validate. Plain text/forward sends snapshot peer/reply/thread before confirmation and retain them through delayed forwarding lookup, avoiding a mixture of captured text and a changed live destination.

**24 extracted-source tests pass** (`/tmp/telegram-analytics-deferred-send-tests.log`): eight existing date-context/inline tests plus 16 guard and callback tests. The new fixture executes production action/comparison/capture/invalidation bodies and actual callback guard lines against stand-ins. It covers 22 relevant mutations, changed-and-restored state, allowed UI-only changes, rich-draft updates, cleanup/weak ownership, reentrancy, duplicate acceptance, stale discard and a changed draft during confirmation. The fixture initially declared its state stand-in private, which prevented compilation of the internal production comparison function; its access level was corrected. Removing only update-time revision invalidation from an in-memory source copy fails five cases with 29 assertions (`/tmp/telegram-analytics-deferred-without-revision.log`). Repository source is unchanged by that negative run.

Changed feature sources pass frontend parsing and targeted whitespace checks. No full app build, native scheduling, payment or network action was run. Analytics remains disabled. Native secret/account-switch/multiwindow races, other preview/editor-owned tasks, confirmation privacy descendants and wider interaction families remain unfinished. The shared rich-draft store also lacks account identity in its key and needs an account-isolation audit; the local revision does not establish cross-interaction mutation history. These limitations remain explicit in the scenario ledger. The full goal stays active.


### Send confirmations and purchase-descendant privacy

Plain-message and inline-result payment confirmations now use a ChatUI-local helper that retains the original privacy context during construction, dialog lifetime and each callback. It resolves at most one known or unknown outcome. Source inspection showed the alert closes before invoking completion; unknown destruction therefore resolves on the next main-queue turn, allowing the same-stack confirmation to win. Independent delivery leases survive reentrant destruction. Cancel/unknown closure discard any attached plain-send media observation without asserting enqueue or request failure. Existing send-state guards still control whether an accepted confirmation can invoke sending.

Insufficient-balance paths use the existing context-aware Stars purchase hook. The Stars screen additionally captures its original context for invoice and App Store actions, with unresolved fallback. Invoice fetch, eligibility and purchase signals retain it through callbacks and disposal; the returned checkout and its inner controller are bound before presentation, retain a separate exclusion lifetime and protect delayed outcomes. App Store lock and delayed success UI preserve the same context. No actual invoice request, payment or native purchase was executed, and no new telemetry or financial behavior was added.

Repository source is unchanged by that negative run. **47 actual context/privacy/signal tests pass** (`/tmp/telegram-analytics-send-payment-policy-tests.log`). Changed ChatUI and Stars sources parse and pass targeted whitespace checks.

These checks do not type-check all feature modules or exercise native StoreKit, checkout, private-derived windows or account transitions. ### Checkout context handoff, independent children and terminal callbacks

Checkout has an additive explicit-context constructor; the old signature remains and defaults to unresolved. The feature hook likewise has an additive companion and observed constructor. Both the observed hook and checkout restrict message-backed invoices by the source peer namespace before construction; the hook cannot overwrite that restriction with an ordinary context afterward. Stars invoice purchase uses the companion. Legacy factories retain protected construction/binding/callback lifetime, while remaining old entry points still need provenance migration to recover ordinary telemetry coverage.

Checkout gives each shipping/card/method/password/web-verification child an independent lease and binds modal plus inner input controller before presentation. It chains existing destruction cleanup. Initial form retrieval, liability checks, payment and authentication signals preserve captured context through callback/disposal. Credential-save/liability confirmations and errors retain separate leases. Terminal completion clears its callback before invocation, with an independent delivery lease, so reentrant closure cannot issue a second result. No claim is made that closing a window cancels a payment on the server.

**Eight extracted-source checkout tests pass** (`/tmp/telegram-analytics-checkout-context-tests.log`). These compile the child helper, factory companion and completion body with UI/context stand-ins. Coverage includes parent/child independence, construction and inner binding, preserved cleanup, secret invoice restriction, legacy fallback, late completion, nil/synchronous factory results, reentrant destruction and terminal deduplication. A negative in-memory source variant removes the source restriction and restores completion-before-clear; two cases fail with five assertions (`/tmp/telegram-analytics-checkout-without-guards.log`). Repository source is unaffected. Feature sources pass frontend parsing and targeted whitespace checks.

### Pending payment-form requests and web completion races

Shipping validation and card tokenization now own a request slot plus synchronized request identity. Context is captured from the bound form before request creation, with unresolved fallback, and survives signal delivery/disposal and any error dialog. Relevant input or save-choice changes invalidate old results; errors and unrelated peer updates do not. Disappearance suspends and disposes observation, while reappearance permits a fresh attempt without reviving old callbacks. Each attempt installs its own disposable before starting, so a synchronous completion that triggers a newer request cannot overwrite the newer subscription. No card/shipping/token/provider-error data is added to analytics. Disposing a subscription does not establish cancellation by the external provider.

The web payment controller consumes its result before calling client code, suppressing repeated and reentrant token/verification output. Disappearance prevents late script acceptance; every navigation delegate invocation still answers under the original context. Malformed and subframe handling and recognized return hosts are unchanged. The existing external-verification path can report `.paid` after a recognized return navigation; that is not a server-confirmed payment contract. Future analytics must distinguish web return from payment acceptance.

They exercise production identity, predicates, subscription-slot setup, guards and the shared signal wrapper with real SwiftSignalKit and model/context stand-ins. **Six extracted web-delegate tests pass** (`/tmp/telegram-analytics-payment-web-tests.log`). Negative in-memory variants fail seven cases with 12 assertions when identity matching is removed and two cases when web-disappearance invalidation is removed (`/tmp/telegram-analytics-payment-form-without-identity.log`, `/tmp/telegram-analytics-payment-web-without-dismissal-guard.log`). The fixture's controller getter was corrected to match the production optional-closure type after its initial compile failure. All negative variants leave repository source unchanged.

Modified feature sources parse and targeted whitespace checks pass. Provider/network requests, real credentials, native WebKit/StoreKit/forms and financial transactions were not exercised. Analytics remains disabled and the goal remains active; app verification remains with the user.


### Rich-draft isolation and cross-interaction send invalidation

The process-global rich-draft key now includes the account record ID alongside peer, optional thread and the existing welcome-message distinction. Absent and zero thread IDs stay distinct. This fixes different accounts sharing the same peer/thread draft entry while preserving matching interactions' in-process reopen behavior. No persisted representation is changed.

The store now provides shared revision observers to delayed send actions. Changed writes invalidate observers for that exact key even if another interaction performs the write and later restores or clears the value. Equal writes remain valid. Clearing removes the draft value; revisions are weakly indexed, stale observer entries are pruned on capture, and finished send actions are not retained by the store. Store access and revision invalidation are synchronized. No account/peer/thread IDs, draft text or media are added to analytics.

**42 extracted-source tests pass** (`/tmp/telegram-analytics-rich-draft-isolation-tests.log`), including seven new store cases and four new cross-interaction cases. The updated harness compiles production store/key/capture code with model/UI stand-ins; it checks account and route-key isolation, matching reopen, no-op writes, empty/change/clear cycles, weak observer lifetime and concurrent writes. Focused in-memory negative variants omit account identity or shared mutation checking (`/tmp/telegram-analytics-rich-draft-without-account.log`, `/tmp/telegram-analytics-rich-draft-without-shared-revision.log`); repository source is not changed by these runs. Modified feature sources parse and targeted whitespace checks pass.

These tests do not type-check the full application or prove native rich-editor behavior. Analytics remains disabled; full app verification stays with the user, and the goal stays active.


### Clipboard acquisition failures before preview

Moved new-message clipboard journey creation before file/permission validation, after recognizing a media-bearing paste. No-media and ignored application input still creates no task, and the existing edit-state factory guard still excludes replacement from composition. File metadata failure is now `file_unavailable` in both paste and attachment-panel composition routes, rather than claiming a missing file. Legacy schema values remain available. ### Drop filtering and composer continuity

Accepted file/media drops and direct Shift-drop now carry a partial-selection size-limit/unavailable-metadata blocker into their original journey. Passive target construction and all-rejected selections with no usable target create no journey. The blocker describes the existing hover-time filter and does not assert a second file inspection at release. No file names, contents, exact sizes or identities are collected.

One deferred action is shared by a set of targets: a changed composer or duplicate invocation cannot start a new task or send. TIFF preparation additionally validates its original composer before showing preview, consumes a result once, discards observation on stale output or controller destruction, and wraps delivery in the original context. Edit targets retain message/session checks and use the shared one-use gate. Blocked chat state applies to both file and TIFF targets. Functional guards work when analytics is disabled or denied.

### Attachment outcome classification and image-picker ownership

File and photo/video attachment panels now use the explicit-cancel companion and a one-use composer action. Explicit Cancel consumes the subsequent nil callback; unknown nil discards, accepted empty selection records `empty_selection`, and full rejection no longer duplicates its blocked step. A changed composer or destroyed owner cannot show a preview. File callbacks use weak controller ownership. The system-image chat route also validates its selection and asynchronous preparation separately, preserves origin during conversion and ignores duplicate/stale results.

The shared system picture picker no longer exposes one mutable global completion to successive requests. An invocation owns its observer until delegate completion; an overlapping invocation resolves nil under its own context without replacing the active request. Completion clears ownership before invoking client code, and old duplicate delegates cannot hide or resolve a newly opened picker. Nil still represents unknown output, not explicit Cancel. The original presentation lease remains live during consumer delivery.

### Preview pending actions and paid-confirmation privacy

Preview scheduling, discard confirmation and submission now use an explicit lifecycle and one-use pending action. A committed submission survives close and marks itself sent before preference or outward callbacks to prevent reentrant duplicates. App-file sends mark committed before their outward callbacks too.

### Preview media-price origin, lifecycle and coarse actions

The price dialog now inherits immutable origin through construction and both controller bindings, with a one-use preview/chat action and a delivery lease. Changed/superseded/closed/committed owners cannot apply stale output; removal invalidates pending actions. Price validation now rejects negative amounts and resolves once before client callbacks. ### Continuous join operation and cancellation ownership

Replaced recursive join setup with an account-owned `JoinChatWebViewOperation`, registered before it starts. It subscribes continuously before bot lookup and initial URL acquisition, requests `requestChatJoinWebView` when the initial URL is absent, verifies bot identity and known destination/query, and preserves the result flags, query and keep-alive signal when applying later URLs. Unknown destinations start unresolved even when the caller supplies an ordinary grant. Learning an ordinary destination cannot promote that exclusion; known ordinary destinations retain the original grant and revocation behavior.

A batch's terminal decision wins before any replacement is presented. URL changes during consent coalesce until the current request completes, and subsequent changed URLs explicitly replace the query tab because the page's current `add` implementation cannot update it. BrowserState's internal open completion is one-use across success, cancel, unknown dismissal, policy completion and disposal. Terminal/cancel paths end subscriptions and remove the exact operation from its owner; user tab closure, account cleanup and same-query replacement stop pending work. Programmatic removal bypasses ordinary fullscreen/admission branches and avoids bulk-canceling other registered queries when the last visible tab disappears.

Corrected the lifecycle issue identified above. A temporary hide now keeps the controller reusable, while terminal close remains irreversible. Show and hide advance a visibility generation before window effects. Hide captures the original window, consumes completion once and ignores window mutations from obsolete generations; the no-layer path also completes. If the controller has been released, the pending close can still order out its captured window without dereferencing the controller's cleared `_window`. BrowserState rejects showing an already closing controller, preventing account focus from reattaching it. Raising a hidden browser restores the show path and handlers; raising a visible browser keeps the existing simple bring-to-front behavior. Show removes previously registered handlers before reinstallation because Window.set appends them.

The browser fixture now extracts the complete production hide method, lifecycle fields, make-key method, show prologue and BrowserState show method instead of replacing controller hide with a stub. Native window/layer animation and keyboard-registration bodies remain stand-ins/omitted. 44 cases pass (`/tmp/telegram-analytics-browser-visibility-tests.log`), seven new: hidden-browser reuse with retained secret exclusion, stale/repeated hide callbacks, terminal close after temporary hide, unavailable animation layer, controller release before animation completion, terminal removal of a hidden query, and unchanged visible-window raise behavior. Removing generation admission and BrowserState's closing-controller guard in memory fails three cases/five assertions (`/tmp/telegram-analytics-browser-visibility-without-guards.log`). The positive and negative processes finished. Production syntax and targeted whitespace checks pass. Analytics remains disabled.

The two lifetimes deliberately differ. Preview owns its pending subscription and closes/disposes it on explicit closure or owner release. Request submission consumes its dialog action and detaches a one-use result gate before closing the parent; accepted work and its privacy context can finish after parent deallocation, while canceled-before-submit/retained Return callbacks cannot start it. ### Invite-link ingress, subscription refill and paid navigation

### Stars purchase request ownership and retained top-up origin

Star_PurschaseInApp now retains its old public signature as a wrapper and adds an optional-context overload. Legacy entries start unresolved; explicit origins are account-validated and message invoice sources additionally restrict the message peer before balance/form construction. The invite-subscription helper passes its original context into this overload. Both returned controllers are bound before presentation. Balance, single-form and payment-bot observation plus entry delivery preserve the origin through real signal subscription/delivery/disposal. Buy restricts the actual payment peer, except the invite subscription's synthetic display peer, which is not a real destination. Receipt and returned subscription peers further restrict success delivery.

A feature-local locked StarPurchaseSession owns one pending attempt across payment or top-up. Missing balance/peer/form cannot submit; repeated Buy cannot overlap an existing attempt. Payment uses a placeholder MetaDisposable installed before request/progress setup and checks ownership again before subscription, so reentrant closure during setup cannot start work. Explicit errors release only their matching attempt before invoking completion, allowing reentrant retry without the older request overwriting the new subscription. Paid state is consumed before feedback, refresh, callback and closure, fixing the old callback-before-procced ordering that could emit cancellation after success. Explicit close, the screen dismiss action and current-controller deinit dispose owned work and report cancellation once; callbacks after those boundaries cannot mutate the parent or submit again. This cancellation describes screen/operation dismissal, not proof that a server-side charge did not occur.

The insufficient-balance handoff binds both top-up controllers and holds an independent origin lease across construction, presentation and child lifetime. Parent closure cannot release that child's exclusion. Child deinit chains the existing handler under the original context and releases only its matching attempt; it cannot revive a closed parent. Balance updates while top-up is open do not bypass admission. This tests and protects the handoff, not all internal Star_ListScreen operations or its native dismissal timing. Root top-up/list ingress, account switching/revocation throughout its own asynchronous work and its transaction/gift/Premium descendants still need explicit ownership.

### Stars top-up screen provenance and purchase-attempt ownership

Investigated the unresolved external-verification branch before changing it. PaymentWebInteractionController's existing externalVerification callback recognizes navigation to a configured Telegram host/telegram.me and reports true; it does not query payment status or return a receipt/joined peer. TelegramCore's Stars payment result distinguishes this URL from a done result carrying updates. Wiring that redirect directly to Stars paid would therefore add an unsupported success inference. No such wiring was added. Verification UI/reconciliation and the checkout callback's existing success semantics remain open requirements; the top-up paid callback below still trusts the existing checkout contract and is not independent proof of settlement.

Star_ListScreen preserves its old public signature and adds an optional-context overload. StarsScreenContext captures account-validated provenance, restricts purpose/gift peers, holds owned requests, and guards actions and deliveries after close. Missing provenance starts unresolved. The purchase-screen handoff now passes context before top-up construction. Gift/top-up options, combined balance/subscription/transaction/revenue observation, withdrawal-availability delivery and entry delivery preserve that original context. Both controllers are bound; explicit close, the screen's dismiss closure and current-controller deinit stop owned work. All Arguments actions use the same closed guard and delivery scope. Nested Buy More receives explicit context; transaction, subscription and recommended-app child presentation binds context and independently retains private exclusion through child deinit, even when the parent closes.

Invoice and Store top-up use the same per-screen StarPurchaseSession. Repeated Buy cannot start overlapping checkout/eligibility requests. Invoice metadata admits one result; empty/error/unavailable-checkout paths release their own attempt without success. Child deinit releases an unfinished attempt, while paid consumes it before feedback/events/closure. Late checkout results after parent closure cannot publish success or revive it. Metadata and Store work are held in the existing payment MetaDisposable rather than accumulating a lock-modal cleanup for every attempt.

Store eligibility admits one result and rechecks the attempt before subscription. A placeholder request protects synchronous completion/reentry. Empty/denied eligibility and empty purchase completion clear the delayed-lock flag, close the lock and release the attempt without success. Errors also clear that flag, fixing the delayed window reappearing after a fast failure. Closing disposes eligibility/payment observation and closes its lock; queued presentation checks the screen's state. The public InAppPurchaseState currently has only purchased(transactionId:), confirmed from the local protocol. One delivered purchase consumes the attempt before finishing transactions, feedback and closure. This is per-screen admission, not serialization across all screens sharing an InAppPurchaseManager; finishAllTransactions remains a global manager operation requiring a wider audit. Native StoreKit transactions may outlive observation cancellation; disposal is not proof of no charge.

### Browser invoice documents and general invoice-link ingress

Browser invoice metadata now belongs to a BrowserInvoiceAction on the initiating page. It captures and account-validates the page origin before request/progress construction, preserves subscription/delivery/disposal, admits one metadata result and holds one active attempt across metadata and checkout. Empty or invalid input starts no request; empty completion releases admission without inventing an invoice result. Metadata errors release their attempt before feedback. Both checkout presentation and error feedback require the page to remain attached to the captured window; a detached/replaced window receives neither stale UI nor an invented protocol response, and the attempt can be retried.

Reload, provisional document navigation, explicit page closure, confirmed tab removal, forced removal and terminal browser closure invalidate pending invoice work. Tab removal explicitly cancels before removal because transitions can retain the controller. Temporary browser hiding keeps the original page attempt. Each request/checkout callback belongs to its generation: an older child can finish privately but cannot send invoice_closed to a replacement document or consume a newer document's attempt. Pending metadata is disposed when the action owner is released. Cancellation of metadata/observation is not evidence that a submitted payment was cancelled on the server.

BrowserHooks keeps the old openInvoice closure and adds an optional context-aware companion plus an observed wrapper. The wrapper account-validates the origin, retains its presentation lifetime, whitelists the existing protocol statuses and admits one final delivery. The installed app bridge passes explicit context into Stars/card factories before construction. It retains the latest checkout status, preserves paid against later cancellation, and sends the final invoice_closed result when the checkout modal is released. A failed attempt while checkout remains open can therefore be retried without prematurely closing the browser invoice protocol. The legacy fallback cannot establish when a third-party hook's checkout is actually dismissed. Native modal/current-controller deinit order remains an integration verification requirement.

General invoice links use executeInvoiceLink: original account-validated context is established before metadata and progress, one result selects Stars/card checkout, and the explicit context reaches the factory. Existing error categories remain unchanged. Both ingress routes select subscription checkout when the invoice contains a subscription period. The link executor's afterComplete(true) still acknowledges dispatch, not payment success. Its generic linkOpen event is still emitted before per-route account validation, so cross-account admission at the executor boundary remains a separate gap.

These separate fixture suites do not establish native end-to-end integration. Production syntax and targeted whitespace checks pass.
