This checkpoint supersedes the previous entry's unmigrated browser/general invoice ingress item, not its payment settlement, verification, cross-screen Store or other descendant gaps. The useful future measurement is how a user reached checkout, which local blocker led to retry or abandonment, and whether UI completion was actually observed. The server already knows payment submission/settlement. No invoice slug, bot ID, amount, payment content, raw failure or new telemetry field was added. Browser invoice_closed is an existing browser protocol response, not analytics. Until a reviewed journey contract distinguishes checkout UI completion, pending verification and server settlement, do not add a second success counter. Duplicate attempts, absent analytics and accessibility interactions are not bot verdicts. The broader interaction/privacy/anti-abuse objective remains active.


### Shared link admission and explicit event scope

Revalidated the preceding generic linkOpen gap against the real Analytics.send and AnalyticsCollectionPolicy.accepts implementations. Important correction: the old executor called send without a scope, and accepts(nil) is false. It therefore dropped ordinary link.open events too; it was not demonstrated to leak a cross-account event. The old link fixture's send stand-in only checked exclusion depth, so its ordinary event assertion was insufficient evidence of production collection. Historical fixture counts above remain historical, but claims about actual ordinary link-event delivery are superseded by this correction. The actual AnalyticsPrivacyTests.testDefaultDisabledAndUnscopedProducersDoNotBuildPayloads already encodes the real missing-scope rule; no core policy change was needed.

Added analyticsLinkContext as an exhaustive switch over the real inAppLink enum. All 38 account-bearing variants validate the supplied source grant against the route's engine/account before any event or route action. Known peerInfo destinations, sticker source peers, group-call peers and story source-message peers further restrict that origin before entry. Missing provenance stays unresolved; secret, revoked, foreign-account and previously rejected contexts cannot obtain a replacement grant. Nine accountless variants preserve the explicit source; peerInfo also restricts its known destination. Such accountless callbacks cannot independently establish an engine/account for later asynchronous work, so their downstream ownership remains a separate audit requirement. Newly added enum variants require an explicit compile-time admission decision instead of falling through a default.

The executor passes this validated context to existing route helpers and supplies its scope explicitly to Analytics.shared.send for the existing link.open event. Only eligible ordinary events can now reach the real collection gate when analytics is enabled; production remains disabled. Link classification and event payload are unchanged. The event describes an invocation with a bounded link kind, not successful navigation or a completed server operation. No URL, username, token, code, invoice slug, account/peer identifier or content is emitted. A username resolution may discover additional restrictions later; that destination must still tighten its own delivery context. Admission is not proof that every asynchronous descendant preserves privacy.

The link harness now extracts the complete real inAppLink enum including its link accessor and analyticsLinkKind classification, as well as the new helper and actual executor entry prefix. Its send stand-in requires a valid nonnil scope and records the supplied grant identity. Five new matrix cases cover all account-bearing variants under missing/private/foreign/revoked and ordinary origins, all ten remaining variants under explicit/missing/private origins, known secret destinations/source messages, and rejection that cannot be recovered by returning to the original account. The entire 74-case suite passes in `/tmp/telegram-analytics-link-admission-tests.log`. In-memory removal of account/peer admission fails three cases/201 assertions (`/tmp/telegram-analytics-link-admission-without-validation.log`); restoring the unscoped send fails two cases/73 assertions (`/tmp/telegram-analytics-link-admission-without-scope.log`). Assertion totals include repeated matrix checks, not independent scenarios. All processes are terminal.

Production syntax and targeted whitespace checks pass. These are real enum/classification/admission/helper source fixtures with real signals but stand-in account/policy/UI/transport; they do not run the full executor body for every route, native account switching, all route parsers, or a real analytics sink. Core policy semantics were inspected and matched to existing policy tests, not newly executed here. Full app builds and native verification remain with the user. This supersedes the preceding browser/invoice checkpoint's generic linkOpen admission item; asynchronous share/login/story/gift/settings/call descendants and the wider interaction/privacy/anti-abuse requirements remain incomplete. Existing event counts alone must not be treated as bot evidence, especially while collection is disabled or missing provenance excludes a route.


### Text, rich-text, command and contact local-send observation

The missing-scope audit also found legacy chatMessageSend producers calling the shared send helper before enqueue. Passing a scope there would enable premature success reports. Added AnalyticsMessageSend instead: an eligible ordinary grant admits a bounded snapshot of existing kind/peer-type/reply/scheduled/silent fields, and the real policy registers it for revocation. Excluded, unresolved, disabled, revoked or contradictory secret-peer metadata constructs no event. Completion consumes the snapshot once under the policy queue; expected count must be positive and both result count and nonnil accepted count must exactly match it. Empty, rejected, partial or malformed results consume the attempt without a success event. Repeated or concurrent callbacks cannot emit twice. Revocation clears pending payload immediately; explicit discard and destruction emit no abandonment event. No input text, message identifiers or additional fields are stored in the observer.

ChatController now creates this snapshot for plain text, structural rich-text drafts, bot commands and self-contact sharing. Its helper admits history/thread/scheduled modes only, revalidates the captured original account/destination context, further restricts a known reply source and freezes the coarse metadata before asynchronous completion. Contact sharing additionally restricts its explicit reply argument and uses that argument for has_reply. Plain text and commands use Sender.enqueue(input:observedResult:), which reports its actual mapped batch count; rich-text and contact requests expect one result. The previous pre-enqueue logging calls for these four paths are removed. Existing send behavior, result-group handling, UI transitions and permission/confirmation gates are preserved. If a confirmation is cancelled before subscription, no accepted result is observed and no send event is emitted.

Clarified chat.message.send in events.yaml and regenerated events.json: it means the complete submission was accepted into the local outgoing queue, not server delivery. Albums still count as one event. This is a correctness tightening for the migrated producers, not a new metric to sum with media-journey outcomes. Older client implementations may have counted attempts; comparisons must distinguish instrumented client versions. Partial submissions are not full-success events and must be analyzed through their task outcome where that exists. Plain text route/recovery/abandonment journeys are not yet implemented by this compatibility event.

16 source fixtures pass in `/tmp/telegram-analytics-message-send-observation-tests.log`. They compile the actual AnalyticsMessageSend, AnalyticsScope/AnalyticsCollectionPolicy, core AnalyticsContext fields/restriction and account-owner capture, ChatController snapshot method, Sender's observed-result wrapper and the actual plain-text result callback. Real SwiftSignalKit drives delayed, empty and disposed subscriptions. Tests cover no payload construction for denied contexts; account replacement; secret/unknown destination and secret reply source; contradictory secret peer metadata; metadata immutability; scheduled/custom modes; incomplete or malformed batches; repeat/concurrent completion; discarded/empty work; and release of a pending payload before the observer owner is released on revocation. The payload-lifetime spy is supplied by the event-builder stand-in. Removing consumption/count/admission/observer protections and reply/account restriction in memory fails nine cases/14 assertions (`/tmp/telegram-analytics-message-send-observation-without-guards.log`). Both runs are terminal. One initial fixture compile failed because its PeerId alias was internal; the corrected public stand-in compiled successfully.

These tests use the real policy rather than the earlier exclusion-depth-only send mock, but peer namespace resolution, account/controller models, generated event construction and the final event sink remain stand-ins. The full ChatController closures, Sender's text splitting/mapping, actual Postbox enqueue and native confirmation/scheduling flows are inspected/syntax-checked, not compiled or run by this harness. No real message or full application build ran. Registry lint and generated-file checks pass using the existing `/tmp/telegram-analytics-plan-venv` interpreter (the default Python lacks PyYAML), as do production syntax and targeted whitespace checks. Registry counts remain 33 events, 438 screens, four menus, eight flows and 17 settings. Analytics remains disabled.

Remaining legacy sendAnalyticsMessage call sites are inline results, app files and media/album submission; they are still unscoped and intentionally not enabled by this change. Inline enqueue returns a synchronous Bool whose relationship to actual local persistence needs a separate contract. Media construction must expose the expected mapped result count before its success event can be restored. Forward/edit/delete/translate/react legacy producers also need outcome and provenance audits. The wider client-route and recovery scenarios, secret-derived async descendants, native verification and cautious bounded anti-abuse evaluation remain active requirements. No missing/partial/cancelled telemetry outcome is a bot verdict.


### App-file, media/album and inline-result local acceptance

Sender's single-media and media-array entry points now retain their existing public signatures as wrappers and add observedResult companions. The array builder freezes messages.count after media/caption/extra-text mapping, then reports that expected count with the first backend result. It never derives the expectation from returned IDs. Custom contents retain their own backend, and empty completion/disposal invokes no fabricated result. Existing return values and the one-result boundary are preserved. The single-media wrapper previously dropped repeatPeriod; both its legacy and observed routes now forward it. The chat media/album apply call also now forwards its received repeatPeriod instead of dropping it. These are parameter-forwarding corrections, not changes to scheduling UI or authorization.

App-file sending with or without a caption and the media/album send callback now create AnalyticsMessageSend from the original account-validated context and complete it through that mapped-count callback. Existing sticker/media journey result handlers and UI transitions remain in place. Full expected acceptance emits one compatibility send event, including for an album with additional text; missing/partial/malformed results do not. The pre-enqueue sendAnalyticsMessage helper has no remaining callers and was removed. This closes the prior checkpoint's three legacy helper call-site items; it does not prove every unrelated sending route uses this compatibility event.

Inspected TelegramCore's _internal_enqueueOutgoingMessageWithChatContextResult: its Bool means a message could be built and enqueue was started; it does not wait for local acceptance. The chat inline apply body now calls the same public outgoingMessageWithChatContextResult factory with the same defaults, then subscribes to enqueueMessages for one message under the retained original context. It restricts the destination and bot source before construction, preserves request/subscription/delivery/disposal scope, and records success only from one complete accepted result. Nil construction starts no enqueue and installs no send transition. The existing transition is still installed after starting enqueue, preserving the original UI ordering. No shared-core/submodule edit was needed, and no bot identity/result content enters analytics.

Nine full media-builder/overload source fixtures pass in `/tmp/telegram-analytics-media-send-observation-tests.log`. They exercise album grouping, captions consumed once, extra text increasing expected count, partial/missing/oversized result arrays, duplicate callback suppression, empty completion/disposal, legacy payload/result compatibility, recurrence/reply/story forwarding, custom contents and empty mapped batches. Real SwiftSignalKit runs with stand-in Telegram models/backend and a deterministic string splitter; these checks do not certify the production Unicode splitter or Postbox. An in-memory mutation replacing mapped count with media count, removing take(1) and dropping single-media recurrence fails four cases/five assertions (`/tmp/telegram-analytics-media-send-without-observation.log`).

The collector fixture now has 22 passing cases in `/tmp/telegram-analytics-inline-send-observation-tests.log`, including six new inline cases. It additionally compiles the actual inline apply body, real AnalyticsPresentation and preservingPresentation implementation. Checks cover construction-before-result, actual result/metadata, nil conversion, empty/partial/oversized results, original private/foreign/secret-destination context, late disposal and revocation before result. A mutation that reports success immediately, drops captured origin and removes signal preservation fails five cases/11 assertions (`/tmp/telegram-analytics-inline-send-without-observation.log`). The actual core factory and backend are stand-ins; source inspection established equivalence to the original core wrapper. Both positive and both negative runs completed. Production syntax and targeted whitespace checks pass. No real send or full app build ran; analytics remains disabled.

The large app-file/media apply closures are wired and inspected, not fully compiled by the media fixture; the production builder and inline apply body are compiled. Native scheduling/payment confirmations, stale composer actions and complete route-to-queue integration remain for verification. The general rich/text collector fixture continues to cover account-policy revocation and accepted-count semantics, without certifying every actual Sender mapping. Forward/edit/delete/translate/react, direct routes outside the old helper, complete text/media journey recovery, navigation and ancillary feature coverage remain open. No extra event, field, content identifier or bot verdict was introduced, and task summaries must not be added to compatibility send counts.


### Forwarding inventory and selected-message picker provenance

Forwarding cannot be counted as one completed server action at picker creation. In ForwardMessagesObject, a single ordinary recipient usually receives a prepared composer draft (possibly after forum/topic navigation), whereas Saved Messages starts enqueue immediately. The multi-recipient branch also starts enqueue, though this object's normal picker declares multipleSelection false. ShareMessageObject has a separate multi-recipient implementation. The existing ChatController chatMessageForward call is before permission/loading/enqueue and unscoped; it remains disabled pending an honest terminal contract. The delayed Saved Messages success UI currently does not wait for enqueue; it must not be used as analytics success evidence.

| Forwarding scenario | Current boundary and next requirement |
|---|---|
| Selected messages from selection actions | forwardSelectedMessages loads the chosen IDs before opening the picker. The provenance/cancellation work below covers this lookup. Toolbar versus shortcut is not yet carried as a route label. |
| A message or album supplied directly by a chat action | The legacy forwardMessages callback now captures current source immediately and delegates to an observed companion; returned/source message peers tighten privacy before construction. Distinct action entry labels remain to be wired at actual handlers. |
| Picker to another ordinary chat or topic | Usually prepares a draft rather than forwarding. Preserve original context through reused/new chats and ForumUI readiness, and distinguish draft prepared from queue acceptance. |
| Picker to Saved Messages | Independent comment/forward signals and delayed feedback currently need owned result observation. Empty/missing/partial results cannot count as full success. |
| Multiple recipients / ShareMessageObject | Freeze the whole target set and source set before work. Any excluded source/target excludes the entire task; handle partial targets separately from full acceptance. Do not infer normal UI reachability for ForwardMessagesObject's programmatic multi-target branch. |
| Per-message scheduled Save to Saved Messages | ChatMessageItem has a direct Sender.forwardMessages route outside the picker. Carry source through scheduling and observe its result separately. |
| Share extension | Different process/account lifecycle; app singleton or picker tests do not establish extension privacy. |

Added ChatForwardSelection owned by ChatInteraction. It retains the original source context before lookup construction and restricts every selected source ID. A placeholder disposable and generation protect reentrant setup, replacement and callback delivery. Duplicate activation for the same pending selection coalesces; one returned value consumes admission; empty selection/result/completion cannot open an empty picker and permits retry. Selection, location, chat mode or normal/report-mode changes reset work before observer notification, including changing selection away and back. Clean, handler teardown and owner release cancel pending lookup. The selected-ID set here is operational lookup state, not analytics payload/storage.

An additive forwardMessagesWithContext/forwardMessagesObserved pair passes the retained origin after lookup, validates the account and restricts every returned message peer. It never reacquires a new ordinary grant after revocation or from another account. The existing direct forwardMessages callback and legacy fallback signatures remain. ChatController uses the companion for both selected and direct messages; report handling still goes to the existing report action. No report/forward event was introduced by this change.

ChatUIHooks.makeForwardMessagesShareModalObserved validates source/account before invoking the existing factory, binds the returned modal before showModal, and wraps getMessages with the retained origin plus every requested album peer through request construction/subscription/delivery/disposal. This establishes the source-to-picker handoff and delayed album lookup, not the entire ShareObject lifecycle. ForwardMessagesObject still lacks an explicit stored origin for its own recipient/navigation/send branches; nested picker emoji/search/topic/confirmation work and async work after picker closure remain unproven. A source restriction discovered only in later returned album data must also reach the eventual action, not be assumed from a scoped subscription.

13 production-source fixtures pass in `/tmp/telegram-analytics-forward-selection-tests.log`. They compile ChatForwardSelection, the actual selection method/observed callback/selection-invalidation block and hook wrapper, with actual policy/context/account-owner capture/presentation/signal preservation and real SwiftSignalKit. Cases cover original grant versus later source changes, mixed secret selected IDs, setup/subscription/delivery/disposal, duplicate click/result, selection-away/back, location/mode/report changes, owner release, empty/retry, returned secret source, legacy callback, foreign/revoked origins, setup reentry, reentrant newer selection, modal binding and delayed album lookup. Removing admission/reset/source retention/picker binding/signal protections in memory fails nine cases/18 assertions (`/tmp/telegram-analytics-forward-selection-without-context.log`). Both runs are terminal. Production syntax and targeted whitespace checks pass.

The real ChatInteraction capture implementation, full update/cleanup functions, ChatController callback installation, modal internals and app bridge are inspected/syntax-checked rather than compiled in this fixture. Account/controller/Postbox/peer-namespace resolution are stand-ins; the real policy tests prove the fixture's revocation behavior, not native screen coverage. No actual forward, report, picker interaction or full app build ran. Analytics remains disabled; no new events/fields, message content, peer identifiers or anti-bot decisions were added. Next migrate ForwardMessagesObject/ShareModalController descendants with explicit context and distinguish prepared-draft, accepted-local, partial, cancelled and unknown outcomes before enabling any forward-success event.


### Forwarding object provenance and Saved Messages acceptance

ForwardMessagesObject now stores its account-validated source context, restricts every initial message peer, and restricts the whole requested recipient set before constructing work. Its old initializer remains available and starts unresolved. The installed optional ChatUIHooks companion supplies the captured source to both object and picker; the legacy factory remains supported. ShareModalController binds the picker, emoji controller and input interaction before child work. Its explicit-context overload cannot relax a private/revoked forwarding object with a fresh ordinary grant; an explicitly private context can still tighten an ordinary source. Other legacy ShareObject callers remain unresolved pending source migration. Binding the input interaction's placeholder peer does not prove ordinary input-helper analytics coverage.

Single-target metadata admits one result, tightens privacy with every returned album message and resolved peer, and stops on an empty source set. Reused and new chats inherit the source before draft update or navigation; topic navigation receives it explicitly and readiness preserves it. ChatInterfaceController exposes the existing implementation through its protocol; the repository has one concrete conformer, ChatController. Programmatic multi-target forwarding preserves the whole-set restriction through individual send construction/subscription. This object normally disables multiple selection, so that fixture is not evidence of a reachable native multi-recipient picker flow.

Saved Messages now observes the first comment and forward enqueue results together before scheduling its success banner. The exact expected forward count comes from the resolved source array; the inspected Sender builder maps one forward per source. A nonempty comment adds one expected message, matching the inspected single-message enqueue wrapper. Every expected result must exist and be nonnil. Empty completion, partial/missing/oversized results and duplicate callbacks cannot produce success. With no resolved source messages, neither a standalone comment nor an empty draft is sent/prepared. The existing 0.2-second feedback delay begins only after complete local acceptance. It does not prove server delivery. The send subscription remains independent of picker completion and retains the original privacy context through late delivery/disposal and delayed UI; no analytics success event was added.

The initial provenance fixture completed 16 tests, and the selection/hook regression suite completed 13 (`/tmp/telegram-analytics-forward-object-tests.log`, `/tmp/telegram-analytics-forward-object-selection-tests.log`). The expanded object suite now passes 25 tests in `/tmp/telegram-analytics-forward-object-acceptance-tests.log`. It compiles the actual ForwardMessagesObject, both picker initializers, chat inheritance method, observed factory and installed bridge, with actual policy/context/account-owner capture/presentation and real SwiftSignalKit. Cases cover ordinary/private/foreign/revoked origins, source/whole-target restrictions, late album refinement, reused/new/topic chats, readiness, owner release, constructor restrictions, public-poll rejection, metadata disposal, empty lookup, and Saved Messages comment/album acceptance. Native account/Postbox/peer namespaces, controller/navigation/UI, Sender and ForumUI endpoints are stand-ins. The complete picker and real core enqueue execution are not compiled by this fixture.

In-memory controls established test sensitivity without modifying production files: removing source restrictions/chat inheritance failed six cases/eight assertions in the 16-case suite (`/tmp/telegram-analytics-forward-object-without-context.log`); removing empty-source/result validation failed three cases/14 assertions in the 22-case suite (`/tmp/telegram-analytics-forward-object-without-acceptance.log`); removing detached signal/delayed presentation protection failed two cases/six assertions in the 22-case suite (`/tmp/telegram-analytics-forward-object-without-lifetime.log`). Removing the constructor source restriction fails two cases/eight assertions in the final 25-case suite (`/tmp/telegram-analytics-forward-object-without-constructor-restriction.log`). All listed runs reached terminal status. Final syntax and targeted whitespace checks pass. Counts describe their actual checkpoint and do not imply full native coverage.

Remaining work: own picker invocation/metadata/payment confirmation across closure and selection changes; reject duplicate/reentrant actions independently of analytics; migrate ShareMessageObject, ReplyForwardMessageObject, story/share-extension and direct Saved Messages routes; carry explicit source into search/forum/Premium/confirmation descendants. The picker completion Bool still means dispatch/closure, and its generic invoke/showSuccess path can report success before acceptance; this change fixes only ForwardMessagesObject's Saved Messages banner. Partial acceptance has no retry/recovery UI contract yet. No forward-success event should use dispatch, draft preparation or the old pre-enqueue call. Distinct actual entry routes and bounded outcome instrumentation remain required. Analytics remains disabled in AppDelegate; no IDs/content, new events or bot verdicts were added. Native verification and the full app build remain with the user.


### Share picker recipient actions and Premium-required lookup

The single-recipient picker callback previously started unowned work on every activation. Added SharePickerAction with one pending generation, a placeholder subscription before request construction, main-queue one-result/terminal delivery, cancellation and permanent closure. It preserves the captured context through setup/subscription/callback/disposal. Admission is independent of analytics consent/enabled state. Error/empty completion releases admission; synchronous next plus completion cannot consume a reentrant newer attempt. Reentrant closure during setup or replacement disposal must stop construction/subscription. Cancellation prevents later picker effects, but does not establish cancellation of a send already accepted by the backend.

Moved the actual recipient callback into selectRecipient. It validates the picker account and target before constructing share.perform, coalesces pending activations, and closes only on the current terminal completion. An error can be retried and retains its privacy presentation until the alert is released. The multi-selection topic-row branch uses the same owner for peer lookup and records threadIds only when the current peer result arrives, before toggling selection. Explicit picker closure cancels both recipient and Premium work and calls the external completion at most once. Selected recipients/comment changes and forum/community push/pop invalidate pending work. These destination reset wiring points are syntax-checked/source-inspected; the extracted fixture executes selection/comment invalidation and close, not native navigation animations.

Premium-required peer lookup has a separate owner and restricts returned peer provenance. The resulting text overlay retains its privacy presentation through its callback lifetime. Learn More admits one invocation while the originating picker still exists and is open, passing the original context to the already observed Premium hook. Closing/releasing the picker suppresses stale navigation while the private overlay remains excluded until released. Inspected TextAndLabelModalController: it stores the callback for its lifetime. This does not prove native deinit timing, nor prevent multiple sequential overlays after each lookup has completed.

The fixture now additionally compiles the actual SharePickerAction, context/recipient/Premium methods, close implementation and selection/comment invalidation block with the preceding forwarding-object tests. It uses real policy/context/presentation/SwiftSignalKit, but fake picker/table/selection/UI/Postbox/Sender and Premium endpoint. The root callback installation, complete notify/viewDidLoad and destination methods are inspected/syntax-checked rather than fully type-checked here. The existing extracted regression suite first passed 39 cases. Removing admission/generation/signal-preservation/close/reset/overlay-lifetime/callback-consumption protections in memory failed 11 cases/15 assertions (`/tmp/telegram-analytics-share-recipient-without-ownership.log`). The final suite passes 40 cases, including replacement-disposal reentrancy (`/tmp/telegram-analytics-share-recipient-tests.log`). Both the final positive and the negative checkpoint runs reached terminal status. Final production syntax and targeted whitespace checks pass.

The generic multi-recipient invoke path remains unowned through peer/unsent/Stars preflight and payment confirmation; it still reads live comment text alongside captured input, dispatches before reporting success, and needs its own partial/retry contract. Search/mention streams and forum/community content loads also need explicit provenance and closure coverage; this change invalidates recipient actions during destination changes only. Direct callers of ForwardMessagesObject are not globally serialized. Legacy share objects still lack explicit source origins. No route/outcome event or bot signal was added. Analytics stays disabled and no native sends, payments or full app build ran.


### Multi-recipient picker preflight and payment confirmation

The generic invoke path now runs its whole preflight/confirmation/perform sequence under the existing recipient action owner. It captures the selected ID set and exact ChatTextInputState before request construction, validates the account, restricts the entire target set, and further restricts resolved peer data. Peer/cached-data/unsent-message/Stars preflight admits one combined result. The pipeline retains privacy through asynchronous work and cleanup; close, changed recipients/comment or destination navigation cancels observation and prevents stale confirmation callbacks from starting sends. Pending single-recipient and generic invoke actions share admission. Operational recipient/input snapshots do not enter analytics payloads.

Validation now uses the captured input consistently. Non-payment blockers are shown before any payment confirmation; paid requirements no longer override a slow-mode blocker. The paid-user count comes from the distinct charged-target dictionary instead of counting every mapped failure. Exact available integer balance is sufficient for the displayed integer price. A cancelled or dismissed confirmation yields no perform, releases admission and allows retry. Insufficient balance opens the installed context-aware Stars purchase hook with the retained origin and leaves the picker available for an explicit retry; buying Stars does not silently resend the old attempt. The balance remains the preflight snapshot, with actual charging/acceptance owned by the backend; this does not certify settlement, native purchase behavior or all balance changes during a long confirmation.

Added sharePickerConfirmation as a cold one-result signal. The actual verifyAlert success/cancel/deinit callbacks preserve the original context, and the child presentation survives picker cancellation until the alert is released. Deinit completion is deferred to the main queue so teardown preceding the confirm callback cannot suppress a valid same-turn decision. Disposed subscriptions suppress old confirmation results. The callback lifetime pattern follows existing alert behavior, but native modal teardown timing still requires user verification.

The picker now waits for the selected ShareObject.perform signal to complete before its success/close UI; errors retain the picker for retry and an owned private alert. This is still a dispatch-completion contract: several ShareObject implementations ignore individual enqueue result arrays or start detached work. No analytics event treats it as complete local acceptance. Partial/missing backend acceptance, per-target recovery and honest generic success feedback still require a structured ShareObject result contract. ForwardMessagesObject's Saved Messages banner retains the previous explicit acceptance check.

The fixture now compiles the complete production invoke method and confirmation helper, alongside the earlier 40 forwarding/recipient cases. New cases exercise duplicate admission, close during preflight, frozen input/targets, empty preflight retry, whole mixed-secret target exclusion, exact-balance consent, repeated/cancelled/dismissed confirmations, changed selection, picker release with private child lifetime, insufficient balance, slow-mode precedence, perform error/retry, and revoked origin before top-up. Real policy/context/presentation/SwiftSignalKit run with fake account/Postbox/Stars/model/alert/purchase/ShareObject endpoints. Full native selector observers, actual core sends/payments and full app compilation are not proved. The final suite passes 54 cases (`/tmp/telegram-analytics-share-multi-tests.log`). An in-memory control removing whole-target restriction, frozen arguments, blocker precedence, exact-balance handling and confirmation lifetime fails five cases/11 assertions among 14 selected multi-recipient cases (`/tmp/telegram-analytics-share-multi-without-guards.log`). A separate control removing confirmation cancellation and selection invalidation fails both selected cases/three assertions (`/tmp/telegram-analytics-share-multi-without-cancellation.log`), avoiding interference from the balance mutation. All final runs reached terminal status; syntax and targeted whitespace checks pass. The first broad negative run exposed an unsafe test-array index after the expected missing-send assertion; the fixture now checks the optional first item and both the positive and negative runs were repeated to terminal completion.

This supersedes the preceding multi-recipient preflight/confirmation ownership and captured-versus-live-comment gaps. Other legacy share objects still need explicit source ingress, search/mention/forum/community content work still needs provenance/closure coverage, and forwarding entry labels/outcome journeys remain open. No new events, identifiers/content or automated bot decisions were added; analytics stays disabled and no native send/payment was executed.


### Message-share per-recipient acceptance results

ShareMessageObject now exposes an internal performWithResults contract; its legacy public perform signature remains a wrapper that ignores the structured result. ShareDeliveryPart describes the expected count and a known accepted count only when the returned array has exactly that size. Exact all-nonnil results are accepted, exact mixed results partial, exact all-nil results rejected, and missing/empty-completion/incorrect-cardinality results unknown. Zero expected work is notAttempted. ShareRecipientDelivery retains comment and forward parts separately; any unknown part keeps the recipient unknown instead of guessing full success. These are operational results containing recipient identifiers, not analytics events or payload objects. They must not be serialized wholesale into analytics.

The message-share operation validates source account and the primary message plus every grouped source ID, restricts the complete deduplicated target set before construction, and refines each resolved peer. A secret primary source cannot be concealed by a supplied ordinary album. Missing inherited provenance stays unresolved. The picker constructor also applies this source restriction before children. Caption attributes, schedule and thread mapping are captured before asynchronous peer delivery. Each target preserves the original comment-before-forward subscription order, but consumes the first actual result rather than relying on completion alone. Empty comment completion becomes unknown and still proceeds to the forward as before; absent peer metadata produces unknown without enqueue. A comment skipped by permission is rejected, so accepted forwards cannot make the whole requested operation successful. Disposing a pending operation emits no fabricated terminal outcome.

Both single-recipient and generic picker paths use performShare. For ShareMessageObject only, success requires a nonempty result set with every recipient fully accepted. Incomplete/unknown delivery closes that attempt with success=false, shows a newly localized explanation to check destination chats before retrying, and never automatically replays accepted or uncertain parts. This is a conservative recovery boundary, not selective retry support. Other ShareObject types retain their previous dispatch-completion behavior pending migration. The earlier Saved Messages banner also retains its separate local-acceptance check. No new forward/send analytics event was enabled.

The production-source fixture now compiles complete message-share perform/context/result methods, the result model/first-result adapter and picker dispatch bridge, in addition to prior forwarding/picker flows. The new tests cover exact/partial/rejected/malformed/unknown results; two targets with separate comment outcomes; unavailable metadata; sequential comment/forward subscriptions; deduplicated targets and callbacks; denied comments; disposal; missing/secret/whole-target provenance; primary-secret/ordinary-album mismatch; complete picker success and incomplete closure without replay. The native ShareMessageObject initializer/forward-info/export-link methods, actual peer namespaces/permissions/Postbox/Sender/alert/localization rendering remain stand-ins or inspected code. The actual Sender builders were previously inspected to establish one forward per source and one comment message; these fixtures do not execute TelegramCore enqueue.

The final suite passes 67 cases in `/tmp/telegram-analytics-share-delivery-tests.log`. One prior regression caught that moving legacy perform construction into a subscription could start work after a reentrant close; the wrapper now checks closure both before construction and before subscribing, and that regression passes again. Localization was regenerated with tools/swiftgen.sh, including pre-existing source-string edits; production syntax and targeted whitespace checks pass. An in-memory control that treats partial/missing counts optimistically, drops comment outcomes and removes source/whole-target restrictions fails ten cases/18 assertions among the 13 new cases (`/tmp/telegram-analytics-share-delivery-without-guards.log`). Both positive and negative runs reached terminal status. No native sends or full application build ran.

Remaining: migrate link/contact/story/inline/callback and direct forwarding paths, preserve context in their detached work and metadata, add source-route labels and bounded journey summaries without double-counting local acceptance, design explicit per-part/target retry when acceptance is known, and verify native picker/account-switch behavior. Legacy app hooks constructing ShareMessageObject still supply no ordinary source context and remain conservatively excluded. Search/forum content descendants and the broader analytics/privacy/anti-abuse coverage remain active; analytics stays disabled.


### Link and contact delivery providers

Added internal ShareDeliveryProviding so the picker uses the same source restriction, structured result and incomplete-delivery boundary for message, link and contact objects. Renamed the result's main part from forwarded to content; it remains operational state, never an analytics payload. Public legacy perform signatures remain wrappers but now wait for the owned result sequence instead of starting detached link/contact sends and immediately completing. The inspected picker call sites start those returned signals; native timing still needs verification.

Link sharing merges link and optional comment exactly as before and observes one expected outgoing message per distinct recipient. The payment preflight now asks the provider for its actual outgoing message count: a merged link/comment is one message, while a contact plus a separate comment is two. This corrects the prior displayed link fee overcount. Contact results retain independent comment and media parts and wait for both; empty/malformed results stay unknown, and cancellation emits no invented outcome. Subscription order remains comment then contact without making contact wait for comment acceptance, matching the previous independently started operations.

Both providers restrict the whole recipient set before construction/subscription and preserve it through disposal. Contact source user identity additionally restricts provenance before picker children; missing origin remains unresolved. Per-recipient topic selections now override the fallback thread for both link and contact payloads; the old implementations ignored threadIds in multi-topic selection. The captured ShareObject attributes apply to both contact messages, including silence, schedule and authorized paid-message metadata. The old Sender.shareContact route did not carry these picker choices consistently; this provider now constructs the same standalone contact media message directly with the existing attributes method. The global Sender helper and other callers were not changed.

80 source tests pass (`/tmp/telegram-analytics-share-link-contact-tests.log`), including 13 new cases. The fixture now compiles complete link/contact perform/context/count methods, the provider protocol, shared picker bridge and the actual ShareObject.attributes body. New checks cover merged content/cardinality, per-target outcomes, missing/malformed results, both contact parts, no-comment contact, actual attribute/type/thread mapping, frozen payload choices, one-message link fee confirmation, source and mixed-target privacy, cancellation, incomplete UI without replay, legacy completion timing/privacy and empty targets. Core enqueue, media/user/peer models, account/UI/permissions and fee settlement remain stand-ins; native initializers, payload serialization, scheduling and payment are not proved. Existing message/forward/picker cases remain green. Production syntax and targeted whitespace checks pass.

An in-memory control removing whole-target/signal privacy, generic provider dispatch, topic overrides/contact attributes and the link count correction fails nine cases/31 assertions among the 13 new cases (`/tmp/telegram-analytics-share-link-contact-without-guards.log`). Both positive and negative runs reached terminal status. No new analytics events, payload content or bot rules were introduced. Legacy hooks still omit ordinary source origins; route labels and bounded journey summaries are not yet wired for these providers. Story/prepared-inline/callback/direct-forward result contracts, private search/forum descendants, selective retries, native account switching and the broader application coverage remain active. Analytics stays disabled; no real links, contacts, payments or app build were sent/run.


### Prepared inline sharing: acceptance and preview ownership

ShareChatContextResult now implements ShareDeliveryProviding. The old core enqueue helper returned a construction/dispatch Bool and started its send independently; this provider instead uses the same outgoingMessageWithChatContextResult factory and owns the enqueueMessages result subscription. One outgoing content message is expected per deduplicated target; comments remain unsupported and do not inflate payment counts. Factory refusal is rejected without enqueue. Missing/incorrect-cardinality results are unknown, exact nil is rejected, and one nonnil result is local queue acceptance. The shared picker applies its existing incomplete-delivery explanation and never automatically replays an uncertain send. This does not prove server delivery.

The provider restricts the source bot and whole destination set before factory/enqueue construction, validates inherited account provenance and keeps the context through result delivery and disposal. Per-recipient topic, silent mode, schedule and authorized Stars metadata are passed into the core factory. Legacy perform still has its public signature but now waits for the result and conservatively excludes collection when provenance is absent.

The prepared-message preview now accepts an additive analyticsContext argument; its legacy overload supplies unresolved context. Both preview bot identities restrict the origin, construction and data subscriptions preserve it, and modal/controller/picker children inherit it. BrowserHooks has an optional context companion for the picker factory, installed by the app bridge; its completion retains the presentation lifetime, re-enters the original context and is consumed once. No fallback obtains fresh ordinary authorization.

PreparedSharePreviewAction admits one picker and one terminal result. Repeated Share With actions cannot open duplicate pickers. Missing window, close and teardown report failure once and close only the owned child; successful acceptance closes only this preview. A synchronous/reentrant factory completion cannot show an already-finished child. The old closeAllModals call is removed. Late child success cannot replace a prior cancellation or teardown result.

The source fixture compiles complete provider methods, the observed picker hook and installed app companion, the preview action, and the actual preview controller/validation/close wiring. Its preview message parsing and row rendering are synthetic; account, engine message construction, payload serialization, enqueue and native modal/window behavior are stand-ins. Tests cover pending/duplicate results, factory refusal, malformed/empty/nil results, options/counts, source and whole-target privacy, disposal/legacy origin, empty targets, duplicate picker activation, acceptance/incomplete UI, immediate cancellation, deinit, synchronous factory completion, callback lifetime, missing window, foreign/revoked/unknown contexts and ordinary grant continuity. Revoked ordinary contexts are checked through policy authorization, not the static privacy classification.

Remaining ingress gap: WebpageModalController's web_app_send_prepared_message still starts a detached metadata lookup and invokes the legacy sharePreparedMessage hook. It needs document-generation ownership, cancellation on replacement/close, original account/source context before lookup, and stale-response suppression. The legacy preview overload remains excluded, so ordinary route coverage is deliberately incomplete. Prepared source-route labels and bounded journey summaries are not added yet. This provider/preview work is not proof of native secret-chat isolation, account-switch UI behavior or complete browser ingress. Analytics stays disabled; no native send, payment or full app build ran.

Verification: all 97 source-fixture cases pass, including 17 prepared-provider/preview cases (`/tmp/telegram-analytics-prepared-share-tests.log`). The in-memory negative control removes source/whole-target restrictions, result lifetime preservation, topic mapping, factory rejection, duplicate admission, immediate child closure, stale-presentation prevention and callback consumption. It fails nine cases/22 assertions among those 17 (`/tmp/telegram-analytics-prepared-share-without-guards.log`). Both runs are terminal. The first cancellation assertion waited until a late result to inspect disposal; it was strengthened to require disposal and picker closure immediately on preview close, and the negative control then detects that defect. Production syntax and targeted whitespace checks pass. These results do not replace the user's native build verification.


### Prepared-message webpage ingress and document lifetime

Replaced the detached web_app_send_prepared_message lookup with BrowserPreparedShareAction owned by WebpageModalController. One page admits one active prepared share; repeated requests during lookup or preview do not create more work. The original context is account-validated and restricted by the requested bot before engine/progress construction and subscription. Metadata is consumed once on the main queue. The returned prepared bot tightens provenance before preview construction, presentation and completion. The new route directly creates the existing same-package preview with its explicit origin; the public legacy app hook remains available for other callers.

The action retains the metadata subscription and weakly tracks its presented preview. Reset invalidates the generation before cancelling lookup/closing the preview, so late callbacks cannot emit success/failure to a replacement document. Preview close cancels its own picker and any pending enqueue subscription. Owner release also disposes lookup and closes its preview without retaining the webpage. Window validity is checked before construction/subscription, before presentation and before terminal event delivery. A setup-time reset or window loss cannot subscribe stale work. Missing or empty-completion metadata produces one prepared_message_failed response for a still-current page and releases admission for explicit retry. Prepared-message IDs are operational inputs only and are not added to analytics. A successful prepared_message_sent response continues to mean complete local enqueue acceptance, not server delivery.

Added BrowserPage.cancelPendingPageActions with a default forwarding to the existing invoice-only method. WebpageModalController implements both, preserving the invoice-only API while the shared method resets invoices and prepared shares. Reload, provisional navigation, confirmed standalone closure, browser terminal closure and tab removal call the shared method. Temporary browser hiding retains the existing behavior; a discarded page/window cannot receive the eventual result. The browser source fixture checks dispatch to the page cancellation boundary; the prepared-share fixture compiles the actual page handler, cancellation/navigation/reload methods and action with production preview/picker wiring. Invoice regression fixtures keep their existing real invoice action and use a stand-in only for the separate prepared cancellation field.

The new returned-private-bot case initially failed at showModal: the preview's internal construction was restricted, but the outer action's presentation still used the earlier ordinary origin. The action now refines and holds the returned bot context across that boundary. This was fixed before recording passing results. The ingress changes supersede the preceding detached-lookup/legacy-origin gap for this webpage event; they do not establish complete native WebKit behavior or cover the other mini-app requests.

Remaining: bounded source-route/attempt/blocker/outcome journeys for sharing, prepared peer-type admission audit, selective retry semantics, native multi-account/secret-chat/window behavior, other mini-app callbacks and the broader application coverage. No new analytics event or bot verdict is emitted, and analytics remains disabled. The user's native build verification remains separate; no real send/payment was performed.

Verification checkpoint: 113 forward/share source-fixture tests pass, including 16 new webpage prepared-share cases (`/tmp/telegram-analytics-prepared-page-tests.log`). The browser lifecycle suite passes 46 tests (`/tmp/telegram-analytics-prepared-page-browser-tests.log`), and link/invoice regressions pass 74 (`/tmp/telegram-analytics-prepared-page-invoice-tests.log`). The in-memory negative control removes duplicate admission, immediate disposal/child closure, returned-bot refinement, signal lifetime and window checks; it fails seven cases/11 assertions among the 16 new cases (`/tmp/telegram-analytics-prepared-page-without-guards.log`). All runs reached terminal status. Production syntax and targeted whitespace checks pass. Progress/native UI, prepared payloads, actual WebKit JavaScript execution and core persistence/transport remain outside these fixtures.


### Prepared-message journey: route and conservative terminal meanings

Added draft flow bot.prepared_share and generated registry artifacts. It answers where an eligible mini-app prepared-share request stops, preserving mini_app_request and the request → metadata_ready → open_preview → open_picker path. Success is complete local enqueue acceptance. Missing metadata is metadata_unavailable; a false picker callback is not_accepted, explicitly combining cancellation/rejection/unknown acceptance because the current callback exposes only Bool. It must not be presented as a precise send failure. Explicit preview cancellation has a cancelled outcome without a result field. This vocabulary describes a mini-app request, which may be automated, rather than asserting human intent.

AnalyticsPreparedShareJourney owns a one-way stage machine with bounded state, one-second timings and one terminal flow summary. It has no recipient, bot, prepared-message identifier, content, exact count or raw interaction stream. It uses the original revocable scope, does no recorder work when disabled/unresolved/private, and clears synchronously on private entry, disablement and account replacement. No temporary, excluded or suppressed event is emitted. The page action creates it before metadata request construction, refines returned privacy before recording metadata_ready, and passes it to an additive preview overload. Preview/picker stages and outcomes are observed from real admission/completion paths. Page reset discards the journey before closing descendants; owner release, missing window and teardown discard rather than fabricating abandonment. The legacy preview overloads preserve their signatures and have no invented journey.

The model tests exercise the actual recorder/hub/registry/validation. The forward/share integration fixture extracts the actual journey state machine and page/preview/picker bodies with a recording flow stand-in; it checks source wiring and revocable context behavior, not production serialization. Secret recipient selection clears earlier ordinary journey state before enqueue/result. A missing enqueue result remains operationally unsuccessful and is reported only as not_accepted. Analytics enablement does not change functional sharing. These summaries complement existing screens and are not extra send events or a total-request denominator.

The strict model build encountered existing TelegramCore Atomic.modify/swap unused-result diagnostics promoted by that package's warnings-as-errors setting. The source was left unchanged; a separate test build with -Xswiftc -no-warnings-as-errors passed and must not be described as a clean strict build. No native app build or real send was run. Analytics stays disabled; registry review/rollout, richer picker outcomes, other share routes, broader interaction coverage and native secret/account-switch validation remain required.

Verification checkpoint: 210 real AppAnalytics tests pass with the warning override, including nine new journey model tests (`/tmp/telegram-analytics-prepared-journey-model-tests.log`). All 122 forward/share integration cases pass, including nine journey wiring cases (`/tmp/telegram-analytics-prepared-journey-integration-tests.log`). The initial fixture compile required removing public access modifiers from the extracted journey to match its internal stand-ins; production access was unchanged. Registry lint/codegen freshness and all 27 Python registry-tool tests pass (`/tmp/telegram-analytics-prepared-journey-registry-tests.log`). Production syntax and targeted whitespace checks pass.

Two in-memory negative controls are terminal: removing preview stage/cancellation/result wiring fails five cases/16 assertions (`/tmp/telegram-analytics-prepared-journey-without-wiring.log`); preserving those stages while removing reset discard and making false picker outcomes optimistic fails four cases/nine assertions (`/tmp/telegram-analytics-prepared-journey-without-outcome-guards.log`). The separate outcome control avoids masking cancellation/outcome defects by deleting an earlier stage. No production files were mutated for either control. These checks support this journey's model and extracted integration, not native application or all-scenario completion.


### Typed share-picker outcomes and callback closure scope

Added a public SharePickerResult companion callback without changing existing Bool completion signatures. Provider deliveries aggregate to accepted only when all recipients are fully accepted, partiallyAccepted when known results contain some acceptance and some rejection, rejected when all are rejected, and unknown for empty/not-attempted/missing/incorrect-cardinality results. Unknown dominates known accepted/partial parts. Explicit closure before a terminal provider result is cancelled; legacy perform completion is dispatched, not proven enqueue acceptance. Both callbacks are consumed by the existing once-only close boundary, and close now preserves the original analytics context through cancellation, modal removal and both callbacks even without a browser wrapper. Revoked/unknown/private origins cannot become a fresh ordinary scope during direct completion.

BrowserHooks has an additive typed companion and observed wrapper. The installed app bridge maps the operational enum to BrowserPreparedShareResult, treating dispatch-only completion as unknown; it retains the original lifetime and consumes a callback once. If only the prior hook is installed, false remains notAccepted rather than guessing cancellation/rejection. The preview maps the typed result into AnalyticsPreparedShareJourney. Existing WebApp prepared_message_sent/failed responses are unchanged. These operational enums contain no IDs or payloads and do not depend on analytics enablement.

The draft bot.prepared_share flow adds partially_accepted, rejected and acceptance_unknown results/steps plus cancel_picker. Picker cancellation has a cancelled outcome without a delivery-result field. Legacy not_accepted keeps its original conservative meaning. Explicit preview cancellation remains separate. This supersedes the preceding typed-outcome gap for this installed prepared-share path; other entry routes, blockers/retries, legacy sources, native multi-account/secret/window behavior and broader app coverage remain open. No automatic replay or bot decision was added; analytics stays disabled.

Verification checkpoint: 212 actual AppAnalytics tests pass with the previously documented warnings override (`/tmp/telegram-analytics-share-typed-results-model-tests.log`), including two new typed-outcome model cases. All 133 forward/share source-fixture tests pass (`/tmp/telegram-analytics-share-typed-results-tests.log`), including 11 new typed aggregation/bridge/callback cases and updated earlier journey checks. All 27 registry-tool tests, registry lint/codegen freshness, production syntax and targeted whitespace checks pass. The in-memory control removing uncertainty dominance, stored terminal results, callback consumption, conservative bridge/fallback mappings and direct close scope fails 9 cases/26 assertions among those 11 (`/tmp/telegram-analytics-share-typed-results-without-guards.log`). All runs are terminal. No native app build or send ran; these results do not establish native closure/account-switch behavior or full analytics coverage.

Audited direct ShareMessageObject, ForwardMessagesObject, ShareContactObject and ShareLinkObject construction. The interaction ledger now separates message and album share controls, selected forwarding, context-menu quick targets, gallery, profile contact, own-information confirmation, profile/topic links, folder/invite links, parsed share URLs, mini-app routes and other feature-link hooks. Menu construction and generic factory creation are not user attempts; server-visible content type cannot recover these routes. This is a source inventory with explicit gaps, not a claim that every listed route is instrumented.

ChatRowItem.share and ChatGroupedItem.share now capture the original chat context at the action, restrict the primary and all grouped messages, and preserve it across factory construction and showModal. ChatHistoryUIHooks has an additive optional makeShareMessagesModalWithContext companion and observed wrapper that validates the account and all sources before invoking a factory. The app installer binds this companion and passes the origin into ShareModalController before emoji/input children are built. UserInfoArguments.shareContact now captures the original profile context before getPeerView and preserves it through the owned one-shot subscription, disposal, resolved user restriction, picker construction and presentation. This preserves secret profile identity when effectivePeerId resolves to an ordinary associated user. The previous shareDisposable ownership/replacement/deinit behavior is retained; missing/wrong-type metadata does not open a picker. Unknown, foreign or revoked origin cannot be refreshed by the newly resolved user. shareMyInfo is a different detached confirmation/direct-send path and remains open for its own audit.

The source fixture now compiles the actual ChatInteraction.captureAnalyticsContext, row/group share methods, observed history hook and installed bridge, plus profile capture/shareContact bodies. Engine/account/UI/metadata and full row/profile initializers remain stand-ins; actual native view state, gallery/search descendants and source-event journeys are not proved. No new route event or content/identifier logging was added. These handoffs make future route summaries possible without relaxing secret-chat exclusion; analytics remains disabled.

### General share journey for the first audited source routes

Added draft chat.share with message_action, album_action and profile_contact entry routes and generated registry artifacts. AnalyticsShareJourney records optional profile source lookup, picker entry, a submission attempt and typed local acceptance. It is a bounded one-way stage machine with one-second timing, one terminal summary and synchronous revocation. It stores no IDs/content/counts and is inert without eligible scope. Profile source is explicitly peer_info; message/album paths leave source absent rather than guessing the chat mode of a reusable row. Other share constructors remain outside this route contract.

The ChatHistory app companion creates a task at the actual message/album action and attaches it to the picker. Profile sharing creates the task before metadata, refines returned privacy before source_ready, and reports source_unavailable only for an observed invalid/missing peer value. Legacy overloads do not fabricate an origin or route. The picker accepts an attached journey only for ShareDeliveryProviding objects, preserving known context at attachment. It restricts the full target set before submitted, then resolves the actual structured result before closure/disposal. Unknown result/cardinality never becomes rejection or success. The prepared mini-app journey stays separate; it is not double-counted through this general path.

Explicit close records a cancellation request before cancelling work. For a pending submission, this is not a claim that already enqueued content was undone. afterDisposed discards unresolved submission state: selection-change/replacement or owner release cannot let a retry result complete an earlier observation. This deliberately leaves recovery after an uncertain interrupted attempt censored pending a fuller retry contract. It does not emit automatic abandonment. Source/target privacy revocation erases the whole task, including earlier ordinary steps, and functional sharing is independent of analytics admission.

The fixture flow stand-in was made generic by spec so prepared and general journey enum values remain distinct; an initial ambiguous-overload fixture compile failure was corrected without changing production behavior. Actual AppAnalytics model tests exercise real registry/validation/recorder semantics, while source extraction exercises the real route/picker control flow with UI/account/engine stand-ins. New questions supported are route-specific local acceptance, explicit picker-close requests, profile source availability and uncertain/partial outcomes among eligible terminal summaries. They are not total-send denominators or bot evidence. Other source routes, picker navigation/blockers, payment/permission recovery, native UI/account switching and the broader application audit remain incomplete. Analytics stays disabled; no native build/send/payment ran.

All 160 forward/share integration cases pass, including 12 new route/journey scenarios (`/tmp/telegram-analytics-share-journey-integration-tests.log`). All 27 registry-tool tests, lint/codegen freshness, production syntax and targeted whitespace checks pass (`/tmp/telegram-analytics-share-journey-registry-tests.log`). The in-memory negative control changes album attribution, makes incomplete results optimistic, drops observed source failure/cancellation and removes unresolved-submission disposal; it fails six cases/18 assertions among those 12 (`/tmp/telegram-analytics-share-journey-without-guards.log`). All runs are terminal. These model/extracted checks do not replace native UI or strict full-application build verification.


### Share picker blockers, payment confirmation and bounded recovery

Completed the previously staged chat.share retry/blocker registry and AnalyticsShareJourney additions, then connected them to actual picker decisions. Admitted Premium lookup records premium_required. Multi-recipient preflight records the last displayed slow-mode reason (cooldown, comment or pending messages), not every internal error or its localized text. Insufficient balance is recorded only after explicit payment confirmation, followed by open_top_up. Whole-target restriction precedes admitted preflight/retry and resolved peers refine the origin before decisions. No identifiers, contents, exact counts or prices were added.

Retry state is consumed once at an admitted preflight or direct submission after an observed blocker or explicit payment cancellation. The 0/1/2_3/4_plus bucket saturates; duplicate activation while an action is pending does not add attempts. Payment opening, confirmation and cancellation are distinct steps. Unknown dialog dismissal or replacement discards the observation, while subsequent functional sharing remains possible. Top-up never implies successful purchase or automatic send. The existing single-recipient shortcut does not run the multi-recipient paid/slow-mode preflight, and prepared-share/other source journeys still need separate recovery contracts; this change does not claim equivalent functional gates across every route.

Inspection found recorder writes after flow.step in blocker/cancellation paths and after nested retry in submitted. A clock callback can synchronously revoke the scope there. Retry/confirmation state is now changed before recording, and submitted rechecks scope/stage after retry, so revocation cannot recreate retained state. Actual model tests exercise disable during each of those boundaries as well as bounded repeated attempts, exact last-blocker semantics, known versus unknown confirmation endings, top-up and private inertness.

The in-memory negative control removes admitted retry/Premium/top-up/dismissal handling and corrupts slow-mode reasons; six cases/13 assertions fail among the eight new cases. All runs terminated. Analytics remains disabled in AppDelegate. No native build, real send or payment ran; native verification stays with the user.

Remaining work includes uninstrumented share source routes, own-information confirmation, quick/gallery forwarding, picker navigation, prepared-share gate semantics, interrupted submission recovery and the broader application/secret-chat/account-switch matrix. Categorical client recovery observations are untrusted, coverage-biased evidence and must not independently classify bots or authorize restrictions.


### Profile share-own-contact action: provenance, ownership and outcomes

Audited UserInfoArguments.shareMyInfo separately from shareContact. The old path detached the target lookup, displayed a confirmation without captured profile provenance, force-cast own contact metadata and discarded enqueue results. Added ProfileOwnContactShareAction owned by the arguments object. It coalesces one pending action across target lookup, confirmation, safe own-contact lookup and local enqueue; each subscription is installed before request construction and generation-checked again before subscribing. Confirmation is consumed once, owner release cancels requests, and shareContact resets the own-contact action so a stale dialog cannot send later. The dialog itself retains the original privacy lifetime while it exists.

The original profile context is captured and account-validated before lookup, restricted by the target and returned metadata, and preserved across confirmation, progress construction, callbacks and disposal. Unknown/foreign/revoked/private origin remains ineligible even when ordinary peer metadata arrives. The own-contact lookup now checks both TelegramUser type and expected account peer identity. Missing/wrong metadata ends without sending instead of force-casting. No unrelated send attributes or payment rules were changed.

Added draft peer_info.share_own_contact with known peer_info source and the route request → target_ready → open_confirmation → confirm → source_ready → submit → local result. It distinguishes observed target_unavailable/source_unavailable, explicit cancellation, local accepted/rejected and acceptance_unknown. Only one non-nil local message ID is accepted; one nil is rejected, and incorrect cardinality or empty enqueue completion is unknown. IDs, contact names, phone numbers and vCard data are operational only. The flow inherits bounded storage and one-second timing. Later explicit actions begin separate tasks; this is not a total-send/retry denominator or a proof of human intent.

Native source inspection found ProgressModalController.close disposes its underlying work without completing the outward signal. An enqueue disposal observer now discards/release admission only when no next/completion was observed. Unknown dialog destruction, owner loss, replacement and this progress dismissal do not invent cancellation, delivery failure or abandonment. No claim is made that disposal reverses already accepted core work.

### Gallery origin and distinct single/group share routes

Audited GalleryViewer share and its chat entrance. The gallery had no retained analytics origin and used an unscoped standalone screen call. Added GalleryAnalyticsContext with account validation and monotonic restrictions. Seven ChatUI media opening sites (file, grouped file, grouped photo/video, general media, message webpage, service media and article media) now pass ChatInteraction.captureAnalyticsContext through an additive ChatNavigationHooks companion and app bridge into the context-aware public gallery overload. Existing public signatures/hook remain available; absent origin is unresolved, never inferred from ordinary message identity. Current chat, initial message, explicit message collection, profile peer and secret type restrict provenance before children are created.

The gallery holds its presentation context before window activation and through actual animated closure. Selected-message updates tighten it, including secret media and private peer namespaces; returning to ordinary media cannot restore the grant. Screen tracking receives this context explicitly. clean/deinit removes the screen owner and privacy lifetime. PiP reopening retains original provenance. During review, group sharing was tightened in the parent as well as the child: a group containing private content leaves the gallery excluded after the picker closes, so an ordinary-looking old parent cannot resume collection.

Gallery share menus capture source message/group/origin and presentation generation. Menu construction is not intent; activating one item records gallery_selection and activating the group records gallery_group in chat.share, both with media_viewer source. The whole source group is validated before picker children. ### Quick-target forwarding: original route, owned operation and typed local outcome

Added draft chat.quick_forward with direct_target/topic_target entry values. Actual target selection starts the journey; menu construction, permission checks and community hover do not. The menu captures the original ChatInteraction/group context and one deferred-action token shared by both direct/topic callbacks. The context-aware factory validates source/account before construction and preserves the origin across later message lookup. ChatQuickForwardAction owns the request, coalesces pending activations, installs subscription ownership before construction, rechecks generation before subscribing and discards unresolved observation on reset/cleanup/deinit. Disabled analytics still permits forwarding.

ForwardMessagesObject.performObserved and the installed app bridge return typed operational results. Updating an already-current destination draft yields draft_prepared. Newly opened chat/topic readiness yields destination_ready: source inspection found readyHistory before a separate invokeInitialAction path, so readiness cannot establish that the initial forwarding action was applied. Saved Messages uses exact local enqueue results and distinguishes accepted, partially_accepted, rejected and acceptance_unknown. Missing source/target, the public-poll restriction, legacy completion_unknown and opaque operation_failed remain separate. No raw errors, message/peer/topic identifiers or content are recorded. Success across these categories must not be summed as sent messages or remote delivery.

The Saved Messages branch now returns and owns both comment/forward subscriptions instead of detaching them. Disposal before aggregate acceptance cancels pending work and prevents later callbacks from scheduling success feedback. Exact complete acceptance retains the existing feedback under the original context. This changes the former outer-completes-before-enqueue contract; its regression test was updated to require ownership. Cancellation cannot undo core work already accepted. Multi-target forwarding remains a separate path requiring further review.

Two additional defects were reproduced before fixing them. Selecting a topic while its parent chat was already open overwrote the parent's forwarding draft and reported draft_prepared; the current-chat shortcut now requires threadId == nil. The new regression failed three assertions before the fix and now preserves the parent draft while opening the topic. A direct ForwardMessagesObject constructed with known secret media could expose eligible context to picker emoji/input children before later metadata restrictions; its initializer now restricts every supplied message for secret media as well as peer namespace. This regression failed five assertions before the fix, including with an explicit ordinary context passed to the picker, and now passes. Both changes preserve functional forwarding independently of telemetry.

Verification checkpoint: 216 extracted forwarding/share integration cases pass, including 20 quick-forward cases and the additional initial-secret-media constructor case. The existing actual AppAnalytics run passes 234 tests with the documented -no-warnings-as-errors override, including three new quick-forward schema/timing/privacy cases; no model/schema source changed after that terminal run. Registry lint/generated freshness and all 27 registry-tool tests pass. Changed production Swift syntax and targeted whitespace checks pass. An in-memory negative control removing pending/disposal/construction guards, original menu context and conservative result mappings fails nine cases/18 assertions among 20 quick-forward cases. It never changes production files. The parent-topic and constructor before-fix regressions independently establish the two later defects. All processes reached terminal status.
