Client routes remain optional untrusted evidence, never a bot verdict or a requirement for legitimate use.


### Selected-forward callbacks cannot relax source or child privacy

Continued the selected-message forwarding audit after the quick-target checkpoint. Found that forwardMessagesObserved restricted peer IDs but not secret media before its context-aware/legacy callback, and the picker factory wrapper unconditionally replaced the child's analyticsContext with its original context. The latter could undo a stricter classification set during construction, including the preceding ForwardMessagesObject initializer fix. The same wrapper protected requested album IDs but did not restrict newly returned messages before a legacy consumer received them.

ChatInteraction now restricts secret media before forwarding callbacks and before ChatController maps messageForForwarding; that mapper can substitute activeEphemeralReplacementMessage. ChatUIHooks checks all initial messages before either factory, and restricts returned lookup values inside their delivery presentation. After factory construction, it validates any existing child context against the account and retains excluded/unresolved/revoked/foreign restrictions. Eligible children still use the original origin rather than a newly acquired grant. Functional callbacks and message values are unchanged; no analytics collection was enabled or route event added.

Four new extracted selection tests reproduced four defects/14 failed assertions before production changes. The final selection suite passes 17 cases; its child-context case now includes a genuinely valid grant from a separate analytics policy, as well as excluded, unresolved and revoked contexts. The wider installed-bridge forward/share suite passes 217 cases, including the new secret-media object/picker/emoji/input regression. Changed production Swift syntax and targeted whitespace checks pass. Every run reached terminal status. Added ChatForwardMenuAction, shared by the main message-menu Forward action, submenu More and existing quick direct/topic targets. Main/More/quick callbacks cannot consume separate attempts from the same menu. Stale revisions and released owners cannot open a picker or dispatch forwarding. The replacement-message menu uses the same helper with both original and replacement messages, preserving secret original media even when the forwarded representation looks ordinary. Main Forward's grouped list and More's single-message behavior are preserved. No journey starts during menu construction, and eligible functional forwarding remains available while analytics is disabled or its scope has been revoked.

Its stand-in ContextMenu initializer initially lacked production's default arguments; that fixture compile error was corrected without changing production behavior. ### Forwarding picker consumes typed local outcomes

ShareModalController now routes single-recipient ForwardMessagesObject operations through performObserved instead of assuming that Signal completion means success. An additive overload accepts the picker comment and payment arguments while preserving the original public two-argument method. SharePickerResult now distinguishes draftPrepared, destinationReady, sourceUnavailable and targetUnavailable alongside accepted/partiallyAccepted/rejected/unknown/cancelled/dispatched. A prepared draft or ready destination completes the legacy Bool callback with true but is explicitly not an enqueue acceptance. Saved Messages uses the existing exact local result aggregation, including comment and forwarded content together.

Non-success or uncertain terminal forwarding results close false and show the existing incomplete-sharing notice, which asks users to check targets before retrying to avoid duplicates. Public-poll restriction remains retryable: its intermediate typed marker emits no Bool, allowing the subsequent operational error to reach the normal alert. Returning false for that marker would be consumed by SharePickerAction.take(1), hiding the error. Pending metadata/enqueue remains owned by recipientAction; closing cancels it and reports cancellation once, without claiming that already accepted content was undone. Failure-alert privacy remains active after picker release until the alert's lifetime ends. The prepared-message app bridge conservatively maps draft/readiness/missing-source/missing-target results to unknown, never message sent.

Seven of eight initial extracted regressions failed 25 assertions before integration. All 236 forwarding/share integration cases now pass, including three additional cases for alert privacy after picker release, disabled-analytics draft preparation and close during metadata. Tests exercise actual picker/provider/bridge control flow, preserving comment forwarding, readiness waits, rejected/partial/empty acceptance, missing metadata, poll error/retry and cancellation. Native menu/controller/account/core operations remain stand-ins. Changed production Swift syntax and targeted whitespace checks pass; no model or registry source changed in this checkpoint.

An in-memory control makes preparation/readiness/uncertainty optimistic, drops comments, consumes poll restriction as a first Bool value and ends the alert privacy lifetime early. Eight of the eleven new cases fail with 23 assertions. This combined control masks the isolated alert lifetime effect because unknown acceptance becomes success before the alert is created; a separate lifetime-only control is therefore recorded below. No production files were mutated by these controls. Logs: /tmp/telegram-analytics-forward-picker-before-fix.log, /tmp/telegram-analytics-forward-picker-tests.log and /tmp/telegram-analytics-forward-picker-without-result-guards.log.

The next required work is carrying an explicit original route from selection/menu/More/replacement/row actions through the common ChatInteraction and app hooks, with source lookup, picker stages, blockers/recovery and these terminal outcomes. Multi-target forwarding and unrelated legacy ShareObject completion remain outside this typed branch. Native secret/multi-account/window verification and the broader app audit remain incomplete. Analytics stays disabled; no native application build, real send, payment, bot enforcement or live analytics transport ran.

The positive run and both fault controls are confirmed terminal. This independently verifies that alert privacy after picker destruction is covered rather than hidden behind the combined control's result mutation.


### Explicit forwarding-picker routes and bounded recovery summaries

Added draft chat.forward_picker and AnalyticsForwardPickerJourney. The scope is deliberately stated in the registry: a picker interaction after source messages are available. It does not claim to count failed/empty/abandoned source lookup before picker construction, all forwarding starts or remote delivery. Required entry_point values are message_selection, message_menu, more_menu, replacement_menu and row_action, supplied by their actual callers. Destination is only chat/topic, retries use 0/1/2_3/4_plus buckets, and only the last categorical premium_required/poll_restriction/operation_failed blocker is retained. Results distinguish draft preparation, destination readiness, local acceptance, partial/rejected/unknown acceptance and missing source/target. No identifiers, content, comments, search queries, prices or raw diagnostics are recorded. Client routes remain optional untrusted evidence, never a human/bot verdict.

ChatInteraction and ChatUIHooks now have additive source-aware companions and overloads that preserve existing public signatures. Selected-message lookup carries message_selection after its owned metadata result; row actions pass row_action; ChatForwardMenuAction passes the three menu entries without refreshing the captured origin. ChatController routes both legacy/context and explicit-source callbacks through one presentation closure, keeps the report-mode bypass, normalizes messages only after the observed privacy handoff and forwards the explicit entry to the source-aware factory. Legacy callbacks/factories without route support remain unattributed instead of inventing a label. nilAllHandlers clears the new callback.

The installed source-aware app factory creates ForwardMessagesObject and its picker under the existing validated source, then attaches the journey using the constructed child's context. ShareModalController accepts this journey only for forwarding objects. Its already typed single-recipient branch records chat/topic selection after operation admission and completes from the verified result. Premium and poll blockers retain retryable picker state. The poll marker resets to picker before its subsequent operational error, so the last blocker remains poll_restriction instead of being overwritten by operation_failed. Explicit closure cancels once without claiming to undo accepted content. Reset/disposal during unresolved submission, owner loss and privacy revocation discard state; no automatic abandonment is emitted. Timing is quantized to one second, and missing/private scopes do not read the analytics clock or retain a recorder.

Verification: all 242 actual AppAnalytics tests pass with the previously documented -no-warnings-as-errors override, including eight new model cases for five routes, preparation/readiness/acceptance distinctions, failures, capped retry, poll/error semantics, cancellation, unknown teardown, no-clock private exclusion, reentrant revocation and owner destruction. All 247 forwarding/share integration cases pass, including 11 journey cases, and all 18 selected-message cases pass with the explicit source callback. All 27 registry-tool tests, registry lint/codegen freshness, changed production Swift syntax and targeted whitespace checks pass. Registry inventory is now 33 events, 438 screens, 4 menus, 13 flows and 17 settings.

The integration fixture extracts the real journey, generated flow enums, menu/row/selection handoffs, controller callback setup and report branch, app factory, premium callback binding, typed picker results and close/reset paths. Native UI/account/core behavior, permission logic and message normalization remain stand-ins; tests do not establish native window or actual send behavior. ### Forwarding retry cleanup must belong to its submission

Reproduced an ordering defect in the extracted forwarding picker with the actual SwiftSignalKit operators. A public-poll restriction synchronously opens its error alert; starting another admitted destination action from that callback can leave the retry pending before the first subscription has returned its disposable. The first afterDisposed callback then called whole-journey submissionEnded(), erasing the newer attempt. Forwarding itself still succeeded, but the recovery summary vanished. The new regression failed four assertions before the fix while the other 247 cases passed.

AnalyticsForwardPickerJourney now provides beginSubmission(topic:), returning a weak cleanup closure correlated with the admitted attempt. ShareModalController captures that closure before constructing the forwarding signal and invokes it on disposal. An old or unadmitted attempt cannot discard the current submission. Current unresolved disposal still erases the journey, while explicit closure and verified outcomes keep their existing semantics. Attempt identity remains local, is cleared on blocking/revocation/finish and is never included in the payload. Existing public submitted/submissionEnded signatures remain available; production asynchronous forwarding uses the correlated closure.

Verification: all 248 extracted forwarding/share integration tests pass, including the before-fix regression. All 246 actual AppAnalytics model tests pass, including four additional cases for previous-attempt cleanup, current-attempt discard, duplicate admission and private/revoked/released-owner cleanup. Changed production syntax and targeted whitespace checks pass. Registry/schema did not change. The first model run stopped because unrelated StoryListContext.swift changed during compilation; that process was confirmed terminal before retrying successfully. The previously documented -no-warnings-as-errors override remains limited to the model test command. Logs: /tmp/telegram-analytics-forward-retry-disposal-before.log, /tmp/telegram-analytics-forward-retry-disposal-integration.log, /tmp/telegram-analytics-forward-retry-disposal-model.log and /tmp/telegram-analytics-forward-retry-disposal-model-retry.log. All runs reached terminal status.

The integration regression uses a synthetic reentrant alert callback and engine/UI stand-ins; it does not prove native alert timing or actual persistence. Unexplained interruption followed by a later action, pre-picker lookup outcomes, search/navigation, multi-target forwarding, other app scenarios and the native privacy/lifetime matrix remain incomplete. AppDelegate still explicitly disables analytics. No native application build, live send, payment, transport or bot restriction ran. The overall goal remains active.


### Forwarding destination discovery without queries or identifiers

Extended the existing draft chat.forward_picker flow with required search_used and the search/open_forum/open_community/back steps. Search usage is one Boolean and at most one step per journey, independent of query changes, clearing, retries or navigation. The Boolean survives the bounded step trace filling up, including first search after its limit. Search predicates are evaluated lazily under policy admission only while the journey accepts picker interaction; private, unresolved, revoked, completed and already-searched journeys do not inspect query emptiness. No query text, length, result list/count, title, peer/topic identity or keystroke history is retained. Observed search state can be programmatic and does not establish human input or selection of a search result.

The installed forwarding journey observes the picker's existing search Promise through a weak callback. Observation is detached on close and destruction; the UI owns its source independently. The analytics observer adds no raw-query queue or delivery operator. Destination-stack changes record admitted forum/community entry and return actions, excluding duplicate top entries, empty-stack return and actions on a closed picker. These are navigation requests, not proof of loading. Forum and community entry now restrict original context before recording/navigation/progress construction and preserve it through async results. Private destination entry revokes earlier observation rather than emitting an ordinary-looking route.

Verification: all 250 actual AppAnalytics model tests pass, including four new cases for search/navigation ordering, search across retries/cancellation, no predicate/clock evaluation in private/revoked scopes and bounded storage with late search. All 253 forwarding/share source integration tests pass, including five new discovery cases. They cover query changes/clearing, duplicate navigation, back/empty stack, typed outcomes after search, private destination entry and origin exclusion, disabled functionality, close guards and observer detachment. The actual real-SwiftSignalKit search Promise is wrapped to count subscriptions; navigation methods are extracted through the loader-setup boundary and tested with a callback there. Native loaders, views, actual search results and window behavior remain unexecuted.

One initial assertion incorrectly expected disposing the analytics observer to dispose the UI-owned Promise source. That run was confirmed terminal before correcting the fixture to count observers and preserve the source. The subsequent lazy predicate tests additionally assert that private/unresolved/revoked/disabled contexts do not evaluate query emptiness. A final summary Boolean was added after reviewing bounded traces, so search usage cannot disappear merely because earlier navigation exhausted stored steps; final model/integration runs cover that version.

### Forwarding activation is explicit and belongs to the admitted submission

Added optional chat.forward_picker.activation with row_action, return_key, number_shortcut, saved_messages_shortcut, send_button and send_menu. The row callback is shared by several input adapters, so it is intentionally not called a mouse click. Explicit adapters override that generic route. Highlighted Return, normal return-key fallback, numeric shortcuts and Command-0 preserve their actual source; no key code, shortcut number, row index or identifier enters analytics. Send-button/modal accept and send-menu callbacks label the submission they invoke; opening a menu or cancelling a date selector does not create a submission. Unlabelled internal callers omit the field, and retries replace it rather than inheriting unknown provenance. Existing public journey methods remain available through a new beginSubmission overload.

The picker captures activation before deferred preflight and passes it to the admitted typed single-target forwarding operation. Duplicate actions rejected by SharePickerAction do not overwrite it. Temporary activation is consumed at the row/submission boundary and cleared before forum/community or premium child setup. An extracted regression demonstrated why: a retry from a synchronous poll-error callback inherited return_key from the old activation. It failed one assertion before consumption was added, then passed. A second test exercises a nested selection at the forum loader seam; another delays send-button preflight, attempts a duplicate shortcut and verifies the original send-button attribution survives.

### Selected forwarding begins before source lookup and transfers one journey

Extended the existing draft chat.forward_picker rather than introducing a second lookup counter. Its required source_lookup Boolean distinguishes selected-message tasks that begin at lookup_source from paths that begin with available messages at picker construction. ChatForwardSelection now owns the source journey with its operation, excludes report-mode actions, ignores empty selections/duplicate pending IDs and finishes an observed empty result as source_unavailable before a picker exists. A nonempty result records source_ready; it does not establish that all selected IDs were returned. Empty completion without a value, reset, selection/navigation changes and owner loss discard without inventing cancellation or failure.

Returned message privacy is checked before recording source_ready or invoking the owner's callback. The original context and same journey pass through an additive ChatInteraction companion, ChatController, the observed factory and attachForwardJourney app bridge. Existing signatures remain. The factory uses context-aware construction when receiving a journey, avoiding a second recorder, then validates the constructed child's context before attachment. Older callbacks or missing attachment bridges discard the partial source trace; a legacy picker may start a source_lookup=false observation. The installed normal app path transfers one object and produces one final summary. Source-lookup strata must be compared separately.

Added exact original-scope restriction at interaction handoff, factory attachment and the picker setter. This prevents a valid but unrelated/foreign journey from retaining observation in a private picker. A negative variant without those checks emits a foreign summary in the private-picker fixture; the guarded path stays functional and silent. Model tests also verify that another ordinary grant cannot replace the original provenance. The constructor, source phase and all later operations remain inert for private/missing scopes, and revocation erases state.

Lookup admission now checks its expected generation after disposing prior work. Otherwise an old subscription's synchronous disposal can start a newer selection, after which the older admission overwrites it. The regression checks the requested ID sets operationally, one surviving newer journey and one final summary. These IDs remain test/operation data and never enter telemetry.

Verification: all 258 actual AppAnalytics model tests pass, including five new source-phase/context tests for one continuous summary, known-empty failure without invented picker/selection, unknown completion, revocation, private origins, picker-only compatibility and original-scope attachment. All 28 selected-source tests and 264 forwarding/share integration cases pass. The source fixture compiles the real lookup owner, observed callback and factory wrapper. The broader fixture compiles actual controller callback installation, report bypass, app attachment and typed picker; it verifies transferred object identity and one trace containing lookup_source → source_ready → open_picker → choose_chat → accepted. Native UI and real core persistence remain stand-ins.

All 27 registry-tool tests, lint/generated freshness, changed production syntax and targeted whitespace checks pass. In-memory fault controls remove source-empty handling, report exclusion, private-return restriction, post-disposal generation validation, recorder-reuse selection and exact-scope validation. Five of seventeen selected-source tests fail with fourteen assertions; separately one of six forwarding-source integration tests fails on private-picker/foreign-journey leakage. Controls never edit production files. ### Coarse source coverage separates available messages from the original selection

Added optional chat.forward_picker.source_coverage for nonempty selected-message lookup results. Complete means exact unique set equality regardless of ordering; partial means a unique nonempty subset; unexpected means duplicate or unrequested IDs. Empty results continue to end as source_unavailable without a coverage value. Unmeasured sourceReady() calls and picker-only starts omit it instead of assuming completeness. This observation preserves the callback's existing messages and order; a subsequent accepted/draft_prepared result still describes the returned batch and must be interpreted alongside source coverage when assessing the original selection.

ChatForwardSelection compares operational IDs only through the journey's lazy categorical sourceReady overload. Policy/loading admission precedes evaluation, and admission is rechecked afterward to prevent restoration during reentrant revocation. Private, unresolved, revoked and already-resolved journeys do not evaluate classification. The recorder receives only complete/partial/unexpected, never IDs, hashes, exact counts, missing-message lists or content. Duplicate/mismatched data can indicate a lookup defect or stale state; the field is optional untrusted client evidence and cannot establish automation.

All 31 selected-source tests pass, including reordered full sets, partial results, unrelated ordinary IDs, duplicate subsets/full sets, empty results and private mismatch followed by re-enable. All 264 broader forwarding/share integration cases pass. The first broad run could not compile because concurrent browser-page cleanup added a locationAction owner; the existing cancellation stand-in was generalized and supplied for that dependency after the failed process ended. No production browser/location behavior was changed or claimed tested here.

An in-memory classifier mutation treats partial, unexpected and duplicate results as complete; one of three targeted source-coverage tests fails with five assertions. It never modifies production files. ### Preview paste admission after disposal

Replacing a pending preview paste can synchronously dispose a converter that starts a newer paste, closes or commits the preview, or changes the chat. The receipt method now checks a non-consuming paste token and the original chat action after installing the new disposable, before subscribing. Result delivery still consumes both actions once. Existing closure-based pending-action callers remain compatible; tokens do not retain the preview. This guards subscription, not construction of the signal supplied by callers.

### Media-preview handoffs validate the receiving context and journey scope

The preview path accepted supplied contexts without revalidating the receiving chat and exposed a freely assignable media recorder. Added an overload that preserves an eligible inherited scope only after checking current account, chat peer, resolved peer, inherited privacy and forward/edit sources. It is used before media-journey creation, at both preview handoff APIs, in the installed ChatController callback before any failure/selection outcome, and at the start of both preview constructors before child creation. Entry/callback presentation leases cover denied and unresolved contexts without disabling functional preview work. Released ChatController owners discard the offered journey.

AnalyticsMediaJourney now supports exact-context restriction. Preview assignment and reads validate scope agreement with its own context; mismatch discards immediately, including for a hidden preview, foreign policy/account or missing context. Replacing a recorder discards its predecessor. Once discarded, restoring the old context or completing pending submission groups cannot revive the task.

### Direct media and recording handoffs preserve receiving-context restrictions

Reused receiving-context validation for sendMediaObserved and sendRecordingMediaObserved, including legacy fallbacks. Missing original context is unresolved. ### Multi-part preview submissions carry an active original context

Added a context accessor and scope restriction on AnalyticsMediaSubmission without changing sender callback signatures. The journey retains its original context locally; the accessor returns it only while submitting under a valid scope, and returns unresolved after completion, discard or revocation. The installed handlers use submission context instead of fresh capture, validate actual destinations before groups and observations, and preserve context through media/rich-text/deferred plain-text signals. ### Composer forwarding sources are classified before accompanying text starts

The returned-message callback previously lacked a privacy boundary, and merely adding one there was too late for accompanying text: the text provider and its observation ran before source lookup. The send now resolves a single source snapshot first, restricts the parent submission by returned source peers, secret-media flags, loaded peer and current receiving context, then constructs text with the validated context. Text completes before forwarding permissions/provider execution, preserving the ability to send accompanying text even when forwarding is denied. A context wrapper covers the combined signal; permission alerts own their context through dismissal. The old unscoped pre-lookup forwarding event call was already denied and has been removed. No new event, wire field or identity is collected.

All 28 preview/direct/multi-part fixtures pass. An in-memory mutation constructs text before classification; two of the 13 composer tests fail with eight assertions. Production files are never changed by this control.

### Composer cached sources constrain early scheduling/payment context

The composer helper checked selected/edited peer IDs but ignored cached secret-media flags and granted contexts before forwarding-source resolution. Once captured, that exclusion survives cache replacement; a subsequent independent action can become eligible after complete ordinary resolution. Cleared forward IDs ignore their retained inactive cache, matching the input accessory display condition. Functional selection, cache loading, scheduling and sending are unchanged. ### Cancel stale composer forwarding preparation before send transitions

Source lookup was unowned and the composer-clearing transition was armed before it finished. Added ChatForwardSendPreparation in the existing forwarding-helper source file, with a single pending owner, captured intent checks before provider construction and at delivery, request cancellation, once-only delivery and silent invalidation. ChatInteraction's existing deferred-intent invalidation resets that owner; controller detachment/cleanup already routes through this invalidation. Ownership ends before delivery so clearing a legitimately submitted composer does not cancel the accepted send. Pending work and text/transition callbacks use weak controller ownership. A duplicate pending preparation is rejected without replacing the first.

The installed text sender now uses this preparation boundary. It arms the history transition or clears the scheduled composer only after an admitted snapshot has passed privacy classification, and scrolls only when sending actually started. Cancelled or empty-completion lookup discards the parent submission and does not clear a newer draft. Functional sender arguments, text-before-forward order and forwarding permission behavior remain intact. This is preparation cancellation, not cancellation of already accepted sends.

### Active reply sources constrain shared composer context

The shared composer helper checked forwarded and edited sources, but an active reply could still grant an eligible context before its source was resolved or despite private source content. It now restricts the reply peer, requires a cached message with the matching full ID and denies its secret-media flag. This moves classification ahead of attachment/date/payment descendants instead of relying only on the outgoing-message event's reply-peer check. Resolved ordinary replies preserve original scope identity. Late resolution or clearing cannot upgrade an already captured excluded action; inactive stale cache does not exclude unrelated new actions. Reply loading, selection, quoting and send arguments were not changed, and no event/identifier/content parameter was added.

One additional case executes actual date/payment wrappers under reply exclusion after clearing the subject, using native-UI stand-ins. All 30 media-handoff fixtures pass. Their new reply case exercises media-journey factory/installed preview entry, and the existing installed text/media entry test now also covers private and unresolved replies. These use context/recorder/provider stand-ins and do not execute full native sender or payment branches.

### Explicit scope exclusion follows live composer metadata into descendants

Snapshot-only contexts did not tighten when later composer metadata became private. Added explicit AnalyticsScope exclusion. Every context sharing that scope now reports excluded, and policy revocation clears recorder state and uses the existing pending-transport invalidation hook. Active AnalyticsPresentation lifetimes observe privacy exclusion separately from ordinary policy-generation revocation; they immediately suspend collection and retain that exclusion until ending. Screen reset reclassifies stored visible contexts, so multiple already-visible descendants remain excluded until each closes. Generic disable/account revocation does not mark unrelated scopes private, and fresh ordinary actions can resume after excluded descendants close. Exclusion is idempotent and cannot resurrect an old origin. No event/schema/content/identity field was added.

ChatInteraction weakly remembers scopes returned by both capture overloads, including the inherited task scope. Its update prefix classifies active reply/forward/edit source privacy before deferred-send cancellation and observer notifications, then excludes remembered scopes when privacy tightens. The registry is atomic and uses a privacy revision to reject a context classified before a private update but registered afterward. The registry lock is released before scope callbacks; references are weak and dead entries are pruned. Contexts from producers that bypass this capture path are not covered by the composer registry.

All 283 actual AppAnalytics tests pass, including six new cases covering derived contexts and active presentations, explicit privacy after ordinary generation revocation, two visible screens closing independently, unrelated ordinary recovery/idempotence, scope/lifetime release, recorder discard and lazy-event non-evaluation. All 314 forwarding/share source fixtures pass, including eight live-update cases. They execute the actual context/policy, capture/restriction/registry methods and update prefix with actual SwiftSignalKit; native state/observer, UI and provider components remain stand-ins. Coverage includes three source types, ordinary metadata, missing reply resolution before pending cleanup, an already-open payment lifetime, weak retention and a controlled registration interleaving. The latter does not claim a full native threading stress test. All 30 media-handoff and 42 date/deferred-action fixtures pass.

### Story previews retain their original send source through delayed submission

If state changes after a batch begins, remaining batches stop and the incomplete observation is discarded; this does not undo or relabel accepted sends as cancelled. Existing previews without a bound source retain their behavior. Original-context/account privacy validation remains separate from this operational source check.

### Emoji insertion, draft synchronization and composer exclusion; accidental-reset recovery

The previous checkpoint covered only the local text-state foundation. Pack-emoji insertion now uses an observed hook/app bridge and ChatInteraction.appendTextObserved. It validates original and receiving composer context, marks excluded/unresolved/invalid-origin text before append observers, and conservatively marks legacy fallback input. Composer classification consumes the effective input marker and revokes existing scopes. Clearing the marker permits fresh scopes without restoring revoked ones; Undo restoration revokes intervening captures.

Same-text/same-entity synchronizeable draft updates retain local provenance and update selection. Unknown replacement/imported nonempty input is excluded; absent/empty drafts clear active markers. Testing the actual Postbox/Engine codec exposed unsupported nested arrays and Int encoding in the earlier foundation. The exclusion field now always writes flattened Int32 bounds, including an explicit empty array for current ordinary drafts. Missing/malformed metadata fails closed for nonempty input. decodeIfPresent handles expected legacy absence without a decoder error diagnostic. Metadata remains local and is omitted from message entities.

During verification an external reset removed tracked integrations. The user confirmed it was accidental and authorized recovery. On resumption the working tree had already recovered the saved autostash fe4c0a993f10122decc5fb0fbf40ec6ba8c96ceb and completed its merge. Compared the saved analytics/model/hook/preview files and docs against the working tree; they matched before the final decodeIfPresent adjustment. Newer UI changes remain, with no unresolved index entries. No blanket restore, stash pop, merge resolution or unrelated replacement was performed by this recovery turn.

Pack fixtures use real context/policy with source-extracted callbacks and fake interaction/provider/UI hosts. ### Native rich clipboard import and inline draft exclusion

### General send-menu freshness and rich-editor snapshot checks

The preceding turn made verified discard/send/scheduling progress (164 cases). Revalidated the general menu implementation: only date completion consumed the deferred composer token, leaving silent and send-when-online callbacks unguarded. All accepted actions now consume that token and revalidate original context; stale date-menu opening is rejected before showing a picker. A weak original-interaction getter avoids keeping an abandoned composer alive solely through menu actions.

The live rich editor is separate from the consumed composer draft. Its send-menu binding now snapshots outgoing blocks and picked media at menu creation, then validates lifetime/closed/sent state, block equality and media dictionary/value equality with a separate single-use action. Changed text/media, removed or additional cache entries and closed/released editors cannot send through that old action. Equal media replacements remain valid. No document/media hashes/content enter analytics. This uses value comparison on outgoing content and Media.isEqual; it is not a deep copy of mutable media internals.
